Perplexity Help Guide

Perplexity’s own help centre, gathered into one screen-reader-friendly document

2026-07-31

Perplexity Help Guide

This guide gathers the official help Perplexity publishes for its answer engine. The text was taken from Perplexity’s help centre on 31 July 2026 and is Perplexity’s own; nothing has been summarised or rewritten.

Perplexity runs in a browser, in apps for iPhone and Android, in a desktop program, and in Comet, its own browser. There is no Alexa skill. All of those surfaces are covered here as Perplexity documents them.

What was left out. The Perplexity API collection is developer documentation, which this collection leaves out everywhere; 1 articles went on that account. Everything else Perplexity publishes for people using it is here.

The sections are Perplexity’s own collections. They are not taken from the trail at the top of the page, which on this site is a product and language switcher rather than a breadcrumb, but from the link each article carries back to the collection it belongs to. Sections are in alphabetical order with Miscellaneous last.

Illustrations do not survive as pictures; where one carried a description it is kept as a short italic note. Tables have become lists. The rating strip Intercom puts at the foot of every article, and its related-article panel, have been removed.

Headings run six deep at most. Level one is this title, level two is a section, level three is an article, and anything below that came from inside the article itself.

Account & Sign-In

Account & Settings

How to Access your Account Info and Settings

You can access your Account Info and Settings by clicking on your Profile icon on the bottom left of your screen and selecting a category from the menu. Clicking on a category will navigate to the main Account/Settings pages where you can review and make changes.

Overview of all settings options

My Account

Under My Account you can change your profile picture and username, and view your account details like your email.

Please note, changing the Full Name and email attached to your account is currently not supported. If you need to transfer your Pro subscription to a different account, please get in touch with pro@perplexity.ai for help.

Managing your subscription

Clicking the “manage subscription” button will open up a new window where you can upgrade or make changes to your subscription information.

Preferences

  • Appearance: You can choose between light or dark theme, or you can match the interface to your system’s theme.

  • Language: The interface language is inferred from your browser and operating system settings but you can change the language by selecting an option from the drop-down menu.

  • Preferred response language: This allows you to select the AI’s response language.

  • Autosuggest: Enable this feature for autocompletion of your search questions

  • Homepage widgets: You can toggle on/off widgets (the little information boxes that show on your Perplexity homepage). Widgets are based off the selection you make under Watchlists (found on the Personalize settings page)

  • AI preferences

  • Model: The AI model selector now lives in the Search input box (if you click on “Choose here” we will direct you to the selector). To learn more about the latest models offered please see this article.

  • Image generation model: Select the model you want to use for image generation, like Nano Banana or GPT Image - 1.

  • AI data retention: Enabling the “AI Data Usage” feature allows for your search data to be used to improve Perplexity’s AI models (Sonar). If you prefer to exclude your data from this process, simply turn off this setting. Note: None of your search data will ever be shared with a third-party service, whether you choose to have AI data retention on or off.

Personalize

This settings page allows you to personalize your Perplexity experience even more.

  • Introduce yourself: You can write a short or long bio to tell the AI a bit more about yourself. This info is used to personalize responses based on your unique preference and info.
  • Location: Enabling your location allows us to provide you with accurate weather and sports info. You may have to enable this at the browser settings level first to give Perplexity permission to access this data.

If you don’t set a location in your profile, responses will be based on your network location. - Notifications:Control notifications for when your Research search has completed running or when your daily finance summary is ready.

  • Finance digest notification
  • Research

Watchlists

Watchlists is where you can select topics of interest that appear as Widgets on your homepage. Currently you can set-up widgets for Finance updates and Sports news

  • Finance
  • Sports

My Connectors

  • Linkedin connector: allows you to connect your LinkedIn account to share your verification and profile data with Perplexity
  • File App Connectors: Dropbox or Google Drive

FAQ

Can you transfer my data to another account?

We cannot transfer data from one account to another. However, if you’d like to keep your sessions, you can add them to a project and invite yourself as a collaborator on the new account.

Can I use Perplexity in Incognito Mode?

Yes, you can switch to incognito mode by clicking on your profile icon in the bottom left corner of the interface and selecting “Incognito” from the menu. Searches in incognito mode are never stored.

Note: If you are not signed into Perplexity, your threads are anonymous by default, so there is no need for incognito mode.

Can I change my email address or sign-in method?

We are unable to change email addresses for existing accounts or create passwords on behalf of our users. If you must change accounts, we suggest you create a new one.

If you have an existing Pro subscription, you can have it transferred to a new Perplexity account by emailing pro@perplexity.ai.

Why is my AI profile criteria not being applied to Perplexity’s responses?

There are technical limitations to what assistants and models can do with custom instructions. It is not possible to “fine-tune” models through the AI profile or add documents or images for reference.

If you do feel that a response is inadequate or inaccurate, despite having filled out your profile info with the right details, please contact us at support@perplexity.ai with some examples and we’ll investigate.

Account Access, Subscription, or Ownership Issues?

Who owns a Perplexity account?

The account always belongs to the person who owns the email address used to register it. Your Perplexity account is permanently linked to the email address you provided during sign-up.

What if I change my email or my institution changes its domain?

Once you create a Perplexity account with an email address, you cannot change that email later. To use a different email, you must create a new account. Please note, this new account will not have any of your previous data, including sessions and projects. ​

If you’re still being charged for a subscription tied to an account you can’t access or that someone else is using, we can help—but only if you purchased directly from perplexity.ai. Include as many payment details as possible so we can remove your billing information. ​

If you subscribed through the Apple App Store or Google Play Store, you’ll need to dispute it with Apple or Google. ​

What if someone else pays for the subscription?

Paying for a subscription doesn’t make you the owner. Even if your boss, company, or friend covers the bill, the account still belongs to whoever has access to the registered email.

  • You can update who pays for the account, but only the email holder can:

  • Change account details

  • Transfer ownership

  • Cancel or renew subscriptions

Don’t remember which email I used or where my subscription is under?

As the account owner, it is your responsibility to actively monitor the email addresses linked to your account and their associated subscriptions. Losing track of which email you used may result in losing access to your account or subscription. Please note that we are unable to provide refunds for any lost access time.

  • Please note: We will only be able to assist with account recovery if you have a paid or promo account.
  • We are unable to change account emails, we can only transfer subscriptions made directly within Perplexity.
  • Tip: Write down or save which emails you use for signups and subscriptions! ​

Can I recover data from a lost account or email?

For privacy and security reasons, we cannot restore or retrieve any data from accounts or email addresses that have been lost or are no longer accessible. Once access to an account is lost, all associated data and conversations remain permanently inaccessible.

Paying for a subscription linked to an email you no longer control?

If you’re still being charged for a subscription tied to an account you can’t access or that someone else is using, we can help—but only if you purchased directly from perplexity.ai. Include as many payment details as possible so we can remove your billing information. If you subscribed through the Apple App Store or Google Play Store, you’ll need to dispute it with Apple or Google. ​ Please note: To be eligible for a refund on purchases made through perplexity.ai, you must contact support and ensure your request complies with our refund policy. ​

​

Account Deletion

How can I delete my data from my Perplexity account?

You can initiate data deletion directly from your account settings, without the need to contact support or submit a separate request.

  • Before deleting your Perplexity account, note that your profile details, preferences, and settings will be removed.
  • Your search history, sessions, and any content you’ve shared are deleted as part of this process.
  • Your account and personal data will be permanently deleted within 30 days. If you change your mind, sign back in as soon as possible. If deletion has not yet completed, signing in cancels your deletion request and reactivates your account. Once deletion completes, it cannot be reversed.
  • Once deletion completes, signing in with the same authentication method creates a new, empty account; your previous data is not restored.

How do I delete my account?

  1. Sign into the account and visit: details or click on the bottom left corner and then select my account.
  2. Scroll to the bottom of the page to findLearn more.
  3. A pop up will appear and you will be able to confirm the deletion process.

Can I delete my search history and all other associated data without deleting my account?

If you do decide to delete your account, all of your data will be wiped out. You can open a new account from scratch using the same email. Alternatively you can delete all sessions and projects or a page one by one. We’ll break down all possible ways to delete.

How do I delete a specific session and/or project?

Here’s how to delete sessions in bulk.

  1. Visit library
  2. Click on the three (…) next to the threads tab and select Delete sessions.
  3. This will delete all sessions, please note we will ask you to double check if you wish to delete sessions. This action can’t be undone.

Here’s how to delete projects or Pages, or individual threads

  1. Click on the project or page you wish to delete.
  2. Click on the three (…) next to the title and select Delete.
  3. Please note we will ask you to double check if you wish to delete the content. This action can’t be undone.

How do I delete a specific file or image, or asset generated by Create files and apps?

We only keep your files for 30 days by default. Learn more about file retention here. In order to delete files, images or material uploaded on a session, please delete the entire session. ​

How can I stop my account deletion?

To stop a pending deletion, sign back in as soon as possible. If deletion has not yet completed, your deletion request will be canceled and your account reactivated. Once deletion completes, it cannot be reversed. ​

Can Perplexity delete data on my behalf?

We are able to process data deletion requests related to GDPR Data Privacy rights. Please note this process only applies to the Right to Deletion right, this means that we will process full deletion and are unable to accommodate for deletions related to:

  • Individual threads, files, projects or any other content generated with Perplexity.

  • All files expire in 30 days.

  • Specific data from specific time periods (I want all threads from x to y date deleted)

​

Account Management and Security

Perplexity can be used without signing in

You can use some but not all features, you will have to sign in if you want to try Pro Search, upload up to three files per day, and save your sessions.

Free Account

With a free account, you have access to all our core features, including five Pro Searches per day, three file uploads, Collections, sessions, and Discover. These features are designed to enhance your experience and productivity.

Perplexity Pro

Learn more about Perplexity Pro here. ​

Creating an account

The simplest way to create an account is by logging in with your Apple or Google account. This provides an easy and secure way to access Perplexity long term.

Alternatively, you can sign up using your email address.

After signing up, a login link will be sent to your email, which will direct you to Perplexity securely or provide a one time password.

Account security and management

Can I create a password for my account?

Currently, we do not support the creation of passwords. If you do not sign up with a Google or Apple account, you can log in using the secure link verification process sent to your email.

Can I change the email address associated with my account?

No, once an email address is registered with an account, it cannot be changed.

If you lose access to your email or need to change it for security reasons, contact support to request account deletion.

Is it possible to transfer data between accounts?

No, transferring data between accounts is not supported.

Can I transfer my Pro subscription to another account?

Pro Subscriptions purchased on the web can be transferred, but those bought via mobile cannot. If you need to transfer a web-purchased subscription, please contact us for assistance.

Can I share my account with someone else?

Sharing your account is strongly discouraged due to privacy and security concerns. It also violates our Terms of Use, risking account suspension or termination. You are responsible for all activities on your account.

To protect your information, do not share your account.

Is a VPN compatible with Perplexity?

Using a VPN with Perplexity is not recommended, as it may trigger frequent security checks by Cloudflare and potentially render the mobile app unusable. ​

How to Change Your Sign-In Method (Email, Google & Apple)

Your account is linked by your email address. As long as you use the exact same email in the same letter-casing for all sign-in methods, you’ll always access the same account. ​

This works across all your devices, so feel free to use whichever login method you prefer! ​ ​

Where did my sessions go?

We do not delete threads randomly. However, due to privacy and data retention policies, some “temporary threads” expire after a period of time. ​

What do Temporary Sessions look like?

Within your history, threads will be marked as a “Temporary thread”, see example below: ​

Here are a few situations where this might occur:

  • Whenever personal context is involved

In some cases, this happens if you attach a file or use a connector. However, if you use the Comet browser, temporary sessions will always expire when your query involves open tabs, browsing history, or triggering an agentic event. This is due to access restrictions on personal context, navigational queries, and data retention policies.

  • Created whilst logged out

Logged-out threads are threads created without signing into an account. These anonymous sessions expire after 14 days and are not recoverable.

  • Incognito mode

Incognito threads are private conversations that expire after 24 hours, use for sensitive queries you don’t need to save.

  • Enterprise retention policies

If you’re an Enterprise Pro or Enterprise Max user, please ask your organization’s admin to check the default thread retention policy.

Lost all your threads

Perplexity doesn’t delete your data, here are a few reasons why this may have happened:

  • Logged in recently

In some cases, you may be logged into a completely different account. Please double-check the email address of the account you’re logged in as.

  • Accidentally clicked “Delete All” instead of deleting one thread

Incognito threads are private conversations that expire after 24 hours, use for sensitive queries you don’t need to save.

  • All sessions missing on a particular?

Sometimes, you might be signed into a different account. Check the email address in your account settings and compare. If you continue to have issues, please contact support@perplexity.ai, share a screenshot of your account settings showing both your email and username. With this information, we’ll be able to assist you further.

  • Are you using Incognito mode?

When in incognito mode, your history is temporarily hidden from the sidebar, however all your threads will still be available to you under the History page.

AI Models & Content Generation

Can Perplexity generate images?

Great question! Yes, Perplexity Pro subscribers can generate images directly within the platform.

Instead of a specific button, you can simply ask Perplexity to create an image by describing what you want in your prompt.

Here are a couple of examples:

  • “Generate an image of a futuristic cityscape at sunset.”
  • “Create a photorealistic image of a golden retriever puppy playing in a field of flowers.”

We’ll let you know if an image request can’t be completed due to content restrictions.

Pro Tip: You can also choose which AI model generates your images by going to your settings page.

These are our available models:

  • Default: Selects the best model for your needs.
  • GPT Image 1: Advanced images from OpenAI.
  • Nano Banana: Google-powered visual creation (Max and Enterprise Max users get access to Nano Banana Pro)
  • Seedream 4.5: Latest Bytedance design engine.

For more details check-out the article Generating Images with Perplexity

Generating Images with Perplexity

The new image generation feature allows you to create custom images that complement your search within seconds.

How does it work?

Add your prompt to the search input and try to be as specific as possible with your instructions. Perplexity will automatically generate the image (you no longer have to click an additional button for generating the image after prompting).

Here are some examples:

What model is used for image generation?

The default option automatically selects the best model for your query from our available image generation models, which include GPT Image 1, Nano Banana, and Seedream 4.5.

Can I choose what model is used for image generation?

You can set your model preference for image generation from the settings menu. Click the “Settings” icon in the user, select “Preferences”, and then choose an “Image generation model” of your choice. The currently available options include:

  • Default: Selects the best model for your needs.
  • GPT Image 1: Advanced images from OpenAI.
  • Nano Banana: Google-powered visual creation (Max and Enterprise Max users get access to Nano Banana Pro)
  • Seedream 4.5: Latest Bytedance design engine.

Can I edit generated images?

If you’re unhappy with the first result you can click on “Regenerate” below the image and we will automatically create a new image. Please note that any regeneration attempt counts towards your image generation limit.

Alternatively, you can add a new prompt in the same session (make sure to include something like “generate an image” in your follow-up prompt).

How many images can I generate per day?

Image generation is available as an enhanced query, with access scaling by plan.

  • Free Plan: Get access to limited image generations.
  • Pro/Enterprise Pro: Unlock access to limited amount of high-quality images, plus additional medium-quality options.
  • Max/Enterprise Max: Experience our most generous offering, with extensive access to high-quality image generation capabilities.

Please note that during periods of heavy usage, image quality may slightly vary.

Can I use generated images for commercial purposes?

Images generated through our service by users in Free and individual Pro and Max plans are for personal, non-commercial use only. For more details, please refer to our Terms of Service.

Users on Enterprise Pro and Enterprise Max plans can use images generated with Perplexity for commercial purposes.

Generating Videos with Perplexity

Perplexity offers the ability to integrate video generation into multi-step creative workflows and projects (such as brand development or pitch deck creation), providing multimedia outputs beyond just video.

Comet’s Assistant further streamlines actions that can be taken with your generated videos, such as posting the video to socials or uploading to a website for you.

How to generate a video

Generating a video is simple! Just add your prompt to the search bar, select a Search Mode and hit enter. A few steps and minutes later your video will be ready!

Once the video has been generated you will see the options to Share, Download, or Regenerate the video.

If you’re using Comet browser you can ask Assistantto publish the video to various sites, like your X or Facebook account (see use cases below as examples).

Example Use Cases

Here are some example prompts used for video generation and links to the videos created:

  1. Brand development: Generate an image of a ready to drink cold brew coffee beverage. Then use it to generate a video ad. Then post the video to X.
  2. Selling an item: I want to sell my old lounge chair (photo attached). Could you generate a photo of it staged in a nice architectural house and then turn it into an snappy video? ⇒ Great, now use the photo and video to list the chair for sale on FB Marketplace. Pickup in 40390.
  3. Real estate: Make a flyover video for this property I’m selling (home) and post it on my LinkedIn along with a nice writeup and link to the Redfin listing

FAQs

What model is used for video generation?

Perplexity’s video generation uses advanced AI models optimized for different subscription tiers:

  • Pro & Max subscribers: Veo 3.1 for efficient video generation
  • Enterprise Pro subscribers: Veo 3.1 in fast mode for efficient processing
  • Enterprise Max subscribers: Access Veo 3.1 enterprise-grade video generation capabilities
Can I choose what model is used for video generation?

No, users cannot choose the model for video generation. The assigned model and quality are automatically determined by your subscription level to ensure optimal performance and resource allocation.

Can I edit generated videos?

No, video editing or iteration on previously generated videos is not supported. Once a video is generated, it cannot be modified or edited within Perplexity.

How many videos can I generate?

We are unable to provide exact numbers, but your current plan includes usage limits designed to keep the platform fast and reliable for all users. Max subscribers receive the highest number of video generation credits each month, while Pro users are allocated a smaller amount. These rate limits may be adjusted periodically. ​ At present, there is no option to purchase additional credits; once you’ve used your monthly allocation, you’ll need to wait for credits to replenish on a rolling 30-day basis. ​ Please note: Perplexity Max subscribers still enjoy extended access to search, research, and create files and apps queries – ideal for advanced users. ​

What are the length and format of generated videos?
  • Max, Pro, Enterprise Pro and Enterprise Max subscribers: Each video is up to 8 seconds long, may include audio through Veo 3.1.
Where is video generation available?

Video generation is launching across Web, iOS, and Android platforms. It is available across all Perplexity modes, including Search, Research, and Create files and apps.

Why did my video not generate?

Sometimes video generation may fail due to content moderation limits and/or copyright issues (you will see a message explaining why the prompt failed). In this case you can tweak your prompt (make it more generic) and try again.

Can I turn a photo into a video?

Yes, you can upload an image attachment to your query to have it be used as the starting frame for your video. The video generation tool can currently only take a single image as an input, and that image will always be used as the first frame of the generated video.

Are there any additional features with video generation?
  • Integration with Comet Assistant allows actions like posting generated videos to social media or uploading them to websites on your behalf.
  • With Create files and apps, you can generate multiple videos at once as part of a larger project.

How do I change to my preferred AI model?

You can change to your preferred model by clicking the model on the bottom left of the prompt input box. If no model is selected it will be named “Model”. ​

Troubleshooting for image generation

Troubleshooting

Why am I getting an error when trying to generate an image?

There are several reasons this might be happening:

  • Your query contains explicit or moderated content, which prevents the creation of the image.
  • Your connection might be unstable.
  • There might be a technical issue on our end. Please file a bug report through our support email.

Why can’t I find a button to generate images?

Thanks for asking! The image generation feature has been improved and simplified. Instead of a specific button, you can now create images simply by describing what you’d like to see directly in your prompt

Keep in mind that image generation is only available for users who are signed in. You will be notified if an image request can’t be completed due to content restrictions.

What is Model Council?

Model Council is a multi-model research feature that runs queries across three AI models simultaneously and synthesizes one unified answer - available exclusively to Perplexity Max and Enterprise Max subscribers on web. ​

How does Model Council work?

Model Council queries three leading AI models at once (such as Claude Opus 4.6, GPT-5.2, and Gemini 3.1 Pro). A synthesizer model then:

  • Reviews all outputs
  • Resolves one combined answer that clearly shows where models agree and where they differ

This approach helps surface blind spots that any single model might have - giving more reliable answers for research that matters.

Who can access Model Council?

Model Council is available to Perplexity Max and Enterprise Max subscribers only.

It is not available to Free, Pro, Education Pro or Enterprise Pro subscribers.

Where can I use Model Council?

Model Council is currently available on web only. It is not yet available on mobile or desktop apps.

How do I access Model Council?

  1. Go to the Perplexity homepage on web
  2. Click the plus (+) button next to the search bar
  3. Select Model Council

Can I choose which models are used?

Yes. Click on “3 models” in the search bar to see available options. You can toggle different models on or off (deselect one to choose another). Each model also has a “Thinking” toggle for deeper reasoning. ​

When should I use Model Council?

Model Council is ideal for queries where accuracy and balanced perspectives matter most:

  • Investment research - Get balanced views on stocks, markets, or financial decisions
  • Complex decisions - Evaluate major purchases, career moves, or strategic choices
  • Creative brainstorming - Surface gift ideas, travel plans, or content ideas by drawing on different models’ strengths
  • Verification tasks - Cross-validate information when accuracy is critical

Why use multiple models?

Every AI model has blind spots - it may overlook context, lean toward certain perspectives or fill gaps with confident guesses. For research you plan to act on, relying on a single model can be risky. ​ Model Council helps by showing:

Where models converge - Move forward with confidence ​

Where models disagree - Know when to dig deeper

How much does Model Council cost?

Model Council is included with Perplexity Max at $200/month or $2,000/year, and with Enterprise Max.

Comet

Comet for Enterprise

Comet is Perplexity’s AI-native browser built for the enterprise. It combines powerful browsing capabilities with AI assistance and the security standards and administrative controls your organization requires, giving your team a smarter, more productive way to work.

Comet is a Chromium-based AI browser with features specifically designed for organizations. Unlike traditional browsers, Comet transforms how organizations interact with the web by providing sophisticated AI assistance directly integrated into your browsing experience while maintaining enterprise-grade security.

Key Enterprise Features

  • MDM Deployment Support - Deploy at scale across macOS and Windows using your existing MDM solution
  • Browser Policy Controls - Configure 500+ Chromium-based policies to customize the browsing experience
  • Agent Permission Controls - Control AI capabilities including website restrictions and action approvals
  • Silent/Offline Installation - Deploy without user interaction or internet connectivity
  • Centralized Management - Manage your organization’s Comet deployment from a single dashboard

Why Use Comet?

Comet transforms how Perplexity Enterprise organizations interact with the web by providing sophisticated AI assistance directly integrated into your browsing experience.

Comet works as a unified AI platform that eliminates routine tasks and speeds up day-to-day workflows. It combines Perplexity’s multiple AI capabilities into a single environment, including research assistance, task automation, Files and apps creation, and seamless integration with the applications that most organizations use.

Comet includes two distinct AI assistants for different needs:

  • Comet Assistant serves as your intelligent reading companion, living in your browser’s sidebar to help with summarizing articles, emails, and Slack chats, providing conversational support, research assistance, and maintaining awareness across multiple tabs. Think of it as a smart reading buddy that makes work easier and faster.

Enterprise Max includes Comet Max Assistant, which provides the most powerful AI assistant functionality available in Comet, powered by cutting-edge reasoning models for reliability and precision on complex tasks. - Comet Agent functions as your digital executor, stepping in to complete tasks autonomously. It can find flights that match your calendar, schedule meetings, fill out forms on websites, manage email subscriptions, and handle multi-step workflows across different platforms. The Agent always checks with you before performing important or sensitive actions, ensuring you maintain oversight.

You will find more ways in which Comet can help improve your organization’s productivity in our free Perplexity at Work Guide.

Enterprise-Grade Security

Comet is built with enterprise data protection at its core. When paired with a Perplexity Enterprise account, it operates under SOC 2 Type II certification and GDPR compliance from day one.

For Enterprise users, no data is ever logged or used to train AI models, ensuring a strong privacy advantage over consumer browsers. All third-party AI partnerships include contractual guarantees that external providers never retain any data transmitted through Comet.

Comet also respects your DNS rules and enterprise security policies. Any sites blocked by your corporate network remain blocked within Comet, ensuring consistent, organization-wide enforcement of security standards.

Getting Started

You can download Comet and obtain your organization token at comet.

Comet for Enterprise is available for:

  • macOS
  • Windows
Trialing Comet

Organization Admins can control which users have access to Comet. This is especially useful for pilot testing Comet with a small group of users before rolling it out across your entire organization.

To enable Comet only for specific users:

  1. Go to Comet setup in your Organization Settings.
  2. Under Enable Comet Access, select Enable for specific members.
  3. Click Configure.
  4. Turn Disable on for any members you don’t want to grant Comet access.

Browser Policies and Controls

Comet supports 500+ Chromium-based browser policies that allow you to customize the browsing experience for your organization.

You can find all the supported policies here.

Agent Policies and Controls

Organization admins can control Comet’s AI capabilities, including restricting what actions Comet’s AI agent can perform within your organization.

You can learn how to manage Comet Assistant permissions for your organization in this article.

Monitoring Comet Usage

Organizations with 50 or more Enterprise Pro seats, or at least one Enterprise Max seat, can enable Audit Logs. Audit Logs provide comprehensive tracking of user activity and administrative actions across your organization.

Organization Admins can also review Comet telemetry data to monitor usage and performance within their organization.

Frequently Asked Questions

How can I manage the data that Comet collects?

Users can manage their data collection settings in Comet Settings, including deleting stored browsing data, saved passwords, sign-in data, and payment information.

Why does Comet request keychain access during installation?

When you install or run Comet (or many other browsers built on Chromium, like Google Chrome), your operating system may prompt you for access to the “Keychain” on macOS. This is a standard security feature in macOS designed to manage your passwords and sensitive information safely.

This prompt is normal when using modern browsers, and responding “Allow” lets your browser manage your passwords as expected. Your credentials and data remain encrypted and protected by macOS’s built-in security.

If you deny the Keychain prompt, you won’t be able to use autofill for saved passwords and may need to enter credentials manually each time.

Is Comet governed by Perplexity’s Enterprise Terms of Service?

When using Comet with Enterprise plans, the same Enterprise Terms of Service apply for accessing Perplexity’s answer engine.

You can find Comet’s Terms of Service here.

Does Perplexity provide a silent installer for Comet?

Yes. Perplexity offers a silent installer for Comet that lets you automate deployment across your organization. For detailed setup instructions, refer to the following articles:

  • Windows
  • macOS
Is Comet compatible with Chrome extensions?

Comet is compatible with most Chrome extensions. However, Comet requires that extensions are installed fresh, and extension data does not transfer from Chrome. This means users need to:

  • Individually reinstall and set up all their Chrome extensions
  • Reconfigure extension settings from scratch
  • Cannot seamlessly import their existing Chrome extension library

Have any questions?

For Comet-specific questions, get in touch with our team at comet@perplexity.ai. If you have questions about Perplexity Enterprise, email enterprise@perplexity.ai.

Managing Comet Assistant permissions

The Comet Assistant is Perplexity’s service that runs alongside the main Comet window in a side panel, giving users quick access to asking questions and executing tasks without interrupting their web flow.

Perplexity Enterprise organization admins have control over the actions that the Comet Assistant can take on behalf of their organization members, as well as the level of access that the Comet Assistant can have to specific domains for the entire organization.

This article explains how organization admins can control what the Comet Assistant can do and which sites it can access for their organization.

Managing Assistant Permissions

Organization admins can enable Comet Assistant to control Comet and perform actions on the user’s behalf, or disable this for their entire organization.

Admins can also allow the Comet Assistant to click, navigate, and fill forms on behalf of organization members, but on a case-by-case basis, preventing the Assistant from taking action without the user’s consent.

To manage these permissions:

  1. Go to the Comet Setup screen in Organization settings
  2. Scroll down to Assistant Permissions

There you will be able to:

  • Enable Comet to Control the Browser: Allows Comet to perform actions on the user’s behalf.
  • Enable “Always Allow” option: Allows Comet to perform actions on behalf of the user without having to confirm.
  • Disable any of these options.

Setting Domain-Specific Permissions

As an organization admin, you can enable the use of Comet Assistant, but disable it for specific domains. You can also set specific domains to Read Only, allowing Comet only to retrieve information from specific domains, without the ability to take any action on behalf of your organization’s members.

To set or manage domain-specific permissions:

  1. Go to the Comet Setup screen in Organization settings
  2. Scroll down to Domain-Specific Assistant Permissions

There you will be able to add or remove domains, and set between the following permissions:

  • Browser Control: Can control the browser and view contents on the specified domain.
  • Read Only: Can only view content on the specified domain – cannot perform any actions.
  • No Access: Cannot read or perform any actions on the specified domain.

Please note that these settings override the global assistant permissions. If you disable the Comet Assistant, but set Browser Control or Read Only for specific domains, Comet Assistant will be able to control the browser and/or view the contents of those domains, depending on the specific settings, and regardless of the global permissions.

Enterprise-grade Security

Comet is designed for enterprise environments, with additional security features available to Perplexity Enterprise users.

Thanks to our learnings from years of experience building and securing AI assistants, we have implemented a defense-in-depth design to mitigating prompt injection, to ensure Comet remains both safe and intuitive to use. You can learn more here.

When paired with a Perplexity Enterprise account, Comet operates under SOC 2 Type II and GDPR-aligned practices, ensuring compliant handling of company data from day one. Enterprise traffic is never logged or used to train AI models, and contractual safeguards with third‑party AI providers prevent retention of any data Comet sends for processing.

You can learn more about how Perplexity protects your Enterprise data in the Perplexity Trust Center, and find out more about Comet for Enterprise in in the Comet for Enterprise overview article.

Computer

Computer Analytics

Computer Analytics gives you visibility into how Perplexity Computer is being used — credit consumption, top users, most-used tools, and more. There are two views available, depending on your role:

  • Organization admins: organization-wide analytics across all members.
  • Organization members and consumer users: personal analytics for your own usage.

For organization admins

Admins can find Computer Analytics for their entire organization in Organization settings > Analytics > Computer.

The admin Computer Analytics page gives you a complete view of how Computer is being adopted and used across your organization.

What you can do

  • Break down credit usage by day — track daily credit consumption across your org to spot trends, monitor spend, and identify usage spikes.

  • Group credit usage by model or credit source — use the Group by control on the Credit usage chart to see which models are driving your org’s spend (for example, Claude Opus 4.8, Claude Fable 5, GPT-5.5) or split usage by credit source. Choose None to view total credits without a breakdown.

  • View the top Computer users in your org — a usage leaderboard surfaces your most active members, helping you identify power users and champions.

  • Learn how Computer is being used in your org, including:

  • Artifacts generated (documents, spreadsheets, presentations, and other outputs created by Computer)

  • Top connectors (which integrations your members rely on most)

  • Top skills (which Computer skills are driving the most value)

  • Top projects (where Computer activity is concentrated)

  • Average task duration across your org

  • Export data for custom analysis — download usage data to run your own reporting, build dashboards, or share with stakeholders.

Who can access it

Only organization admins can view this page. If you don’t see the analytics section, reach out to an existing admin in your organization to confirm your role.

Computer Analytics API

Enterprise organizations can also access Computer usage analytics programmatically via the Computer Analytics API. The API returns the same metrics shown in the admin dashboard — credits, connectors, artifacts, skills, projects, workflows, and task durations — as bucketed time series that can be pulled into BI tools or internal reporting. Analytics requests are free and don’t consume API credits.

Org admins can enable the API and generate a key from Organization settings > Computer > Analytics. The key is organization-scoped, analytics-only, and managed by any org admin. For setup steps, endpoints, parameters, and response details, see the full Computer Analytics API documentation.

For organization members and Perplexity Pro and Max users

Individual members can also see their personal analytics in All settings > Analytics. This is also available to Perplexity Pro and Max users (not on an Enterprise organization).

The personal Computer Analytics page helps you understand your own Computer usage — useful for managing your credit budget and reviewing how Computer fits into your workflow.

What you can do

  • Break down your own credit usage by day — see exactly how your credits have been spent over time.

  • Group your credit usage by model or credit source — use the Group by control on the Credit usage chart to see which models account for your credit spend, or split it by credit source. Choose None to view total credits without a breakdown.

  • Explore how you’re using Computer, including:

  • Artifacts generated by your tasks

  • Top connectors you’ve used

  • Top skills you’ve invoked

  • Top projects where you run Computer

  • Average task duration for your tasks

Who can access it

Individuals using Perplexity Pro and Max, and organization members, can view their personal analytics page.

FAQ

How do I break down credit usage by model?

First, open the Credit usage chart. Org admins can find it in Organization settings > Analytics > Computer. Individual members and Perplexity Pro and Max users can find it in All settings > Analytics.

On the Credit usage chart, open the Group by control in the top-right corner and select Model. The chart then shows a stacked breakdown of credits by model for each day, with a colour-coded legend below the chart. You can also group by Credit Source, or select None to see total credit usage without a breakdown. This is available in both the org-wide admin view and the personal analytics view.

Can org members see other members’ usage?

No. The personal analytics page only shows your own activity. Org-wide visibility, including the leaderboard, is restricted to admins.

How often is the data refreshed?

Data is refreshed once per day, so the most recent complete data is typically from the previous day.

Where can I learn more about credits?

See the How Credits Work for Enterprise organizations — Member Guide article (or How Credits Work on Perplexity for Perplexity Pro and Max users not on Enterprise organizations) for details on how credits are calculated and what counts toward your usage.

Computer for Email

Computer for Email lets you assign tasks to Perplexity Computer from your email. You send a task to a dedicated address, Computer reads the full email context, runs the task, and replies in the same thread with the result. It is available to anyone with access to Computer.

Note: Computer for Email is in early access to select enterprises right now. If you would like your enterprise to get access, email jeff.grimes@perplexity.ai.

Overview

The tasks you can run in Computer on the web can also be run from email. You send a request to Computer’s email address, and it starts a task using the same connectors, settings, permissions, and Memory you already have, then replies in the same thread. Computer can return deliverables such as PDFs, spreadsheets, images, and videos as email attachments, and each email task creates a session you can open later on web or mobile. This article covers how to use it, what you get back, how privacy works, and other details.

Prerequisites

To use Computer for Email, users need:

  • A Perplexity plan with Perplexity Computer.
  • An email account with the same email address they use to sign into their Perplexity account.
  • Enterprise users must have access enabled for their account by admins.

Why use Computer for Email?

  • Uses the context in your inbox. When the details are already in an email thread, you can forward or reply in place instead of copying them into Computer. Computer takes in the full context of the email thread automatically, without being asked.
  • Get real deliverables back. When you request a PDF, spreadsheet, image, video, or other artifact, Computer returns it as an email attachment.
  • Keep your connectors and settings. Computer for Email uses the same connectors, settings, and permissions as Computer on the web. There is nothing new to set up.
  • Works from any device. You can send a task from your phone’s mail app or any email client, without the Perplexity app.
  • Stays in sync. Each email task becomes a session in your Computer view, so you can continue it on web or mobile.

Getting started

  • To start a task, send an email to computer@perplexity.com from the email address tied to your Perplexity account.
  • To give Computer context, include your request in the subject and body, forward an existing thread, or attach files.
  • To get your result, watch for Computer’s reply in the same email thread.

There is nothing to install or configure.

Three ways to use it

  • Email a task directly. Write your request in a new email to computer@perplexity.com, just like you would type it into Computer on the web. For example: “Build a 5-year unlevered DCF for NVDA and send it back as an Excel model.”
  • Forward an email or thread. When the context you need is already in your inbox, forward the message to computer@perplexity.com and tell Computer what to do with it — for example, “Summarize this thread and draft a reply that answers their open questions.”
  • Add Computer to a thread. CC or add computer@perplexity.com to an existing thread and ask it to handle the follow-up work with the full conversation as context.

Attaching files

  • To add files, attach them to your email and reference them in your request. Computer takes attachments into context the same way uploads work in Computer on the web.
  • To act on a file, tell Computer what you want done with it — for example, attach a CSV and ask Computer to clean the data, remove duplicate rows, and send back a formatted spreadsheet.

What you get back

  • First, a confirmation reply. Computer replies to confirm it is working on your request. This first reply includes a Perplexity link you can open to view the session and its steps. Computer sends a second reply in the same thread once the request is complete.
  • A reply in the same thread. Computer replies in the sender’s email thread, but only to the verified sender.
  • Attachments for deliverables. Deliverables such as PDFs, spreadsheets, images, videos, and other artifacts are returned as email attachments.
  • A session you can revisit. Every email task creates a session in your Computer session view, which you can open on web or mobile to review the steps and continue the task.

Computer’s first reply, confirming it is working on the task:

Computer’s second reply, delivering the finished result as an attachment:

What Computer for Email can process

When you invoke Computer by email, Computer may process:

  • Sender and recipient headers.
  • Subject and body.
  • Forwarded or quoted email context.
  • Attachments.
  • Message IDs, references, thread metadata, and delivery metadata.
  • Generated replies and generated attachments.
  • Computer task and session metadata.

Security, privacy, and permissions

  • Same security model. Computer for Email follows the same Enterprise Computer security and data-handling model as Computer on the web. It runs tasks for verified senders under their existing Perplexity accounts, using the same connectors, settings, permissions, and Memory, with nothing additional to connect or set up.
  • Inbound email is verified. Before a task is created, the message must be addressed to Computer, pass sender-authentication checks, come from an eligible sender, and map to an existing Perplexity user with Computer access.
  • What Computer receives. A task may include the sender’s subject and body, forwarded or quoted context, attachments, and threading metadata.
  • Only the sender’s instructions are trusted. The verified sender’s instructions are treated as the request. Forwarded or quoted third-party content and attachments provide context, but are not treated as trusted instructions.
  • Replies go only to you. Computer replies only to the verified sender, even when you invoke it on a thread with many people. Nobody else on the thread receives Computer’s reply.
  • Share sessions separately. You can share the Computer session using normal Computer sharing controls. Email replies remain sender-only.
  • Shared Enterprise security controls apply. Perplexity processes these emails through its internal services, with no third-party mail processor involved. No additional abuse-scanning workflow is added for email. Computer for Email uses the same safety, retention, and audit controls as Computer on the web.
  • Human access follows standard controls. Computer for Email does not introduce a new human-review workflow for email content. Any human access follows Perplexity’s standard authorized production, support, security, legal, and compliance access-control and logging processes.
  • Secure infrastructure. Computer is built on Perplexity’s secure, SOC 2 Type II-certified infrastructure.
  • End-to-end encryption. Data is encrypted in transit and at rest.
  • No AI training on your data. Email messages, attachments, and enterprise data are not used for model training.
  • User-level permissions. Computer for Email runs tasks under the verified sender’s Perplexity account and uses that user’s existing Computer connectors, settings, permissions, and Memory.
  • Audit logs. Admins in eligible organizations can view Computer activity within the Audit Logs feature.

Frequently asked questions

How do I follow up on an email task?

Reply in the same thread after Computer responds. Computer retains the context of the task, so you can refine the request, ask for changes, or add a new instruction without starting over.

How does Computer verify the sender?

Before creating a task, Computer for Email checks that the message is addressed to Computer, passes sender-authentication checks, is not Bcc-only, is not auto-generated/bulk/list/bounce/loop mail, and comes from an eligible sender that maps to an existing Perplexity user with Computer access.

Does Computer treat forwarded email as instructions?

The verified sender’s subject and body are treated as the request. Forwarded or quoted third-party content and attachments are included as context, but are not trusted as instructions over the verified sender, system instructions, or safety policy.

Which email address do I use?

computer@perplexity.com — send from the address tied to your Perplexity account so Computer can match your connectors and settings.

Will everyone on a thread see Computer’s reply?

No. Computer replies only to the verified sender, even when invoked on a group thread. This is not currently configurable.

Can Computer send me files?

Yes. Artifacts Computer creates — PDF, spreadsheet, image, video, and others — are returned as email attachments.

Can I attach my own files?

Yes. Attach files to your email and Computer will take them into context the same way uploads work in Computer on the web.

Where do my email tasks show up?

In your normal Computer session view, accessible from web and mobile.

Publishing your website to the web

Once Computer builds a website or web app for you, you can publish it to a live, shareable URL — either on a customizable Perplexity-hosted pplx.app address, or to your own custom domain via Vercel. This is available to anyone who can create websites with Computer, on web.

Overview

Building a website with Computer starts in a preview, visible only to you. Publishing turns that preview into a live site others can open in a browser — no hosting setup, deployment steps, or DNS configuration required. You choose exactly who can see it, from a private link just for you to a page that’s public to everyone, and you can keep iterating and re-publishing to the same URL at any time. This article explains the two ways to publish, how to control access, and how to manage a published site over time.

Why publish your website?

  • Share a live link. Turn a preview into a real, working site others can open in any browser — no install or setup.
  • Control who can see it. Set a published pplx.app site to be visible to just you, your whole organization, specific people, or everyone.
  • Keep iterating. Ask Computer to make changes and re-publish to the same URL, so your link always points to the latest version.
  • Go custom when you need to. Publish to Vercel to use your own custom domain and hosting infrastructure.

Two ways to publish

When you view a website Computer has built, the publish view offers two paths:

  • Publish (Perplexity): Publishes your site to a Perplexity-hosted pplx.app URL. These sites are hosted by Perplexity and support visibility controls, so a site can be published and still stay private to you or your organization.
  • Publish with Vercel: Deploys your site to Vercel through the Vercel connector. Choose this if you want a custom domain, customized infrastructure, or other Vercel-managed settings.
Publishing in Perplexity
  • To publish, open the website preview and select Publish in the publish view.
  • To set who can see it, choose a visibility level before or after publishing.
  • To update a published site, ask Computer to make changes — it re-publishes to the same pplx.app URL.
  • To take a site down, use the Unpublish option and confirm when prompted.
Visibility and access
  • Default visibility: Sites published by organization users are visible only to your organization by default. Consumer-published sites are public to everyone by default.
  • Visibility options: You can set a published pplx.app site to be private (just you), organization-visible, visible to specific people, or public to everyone.
  • Request and grant access: If someone without access opens a private pplx.app link, they can request access by email, and you can grant it.
  • Enterprise access control: Because visibility is enforced on published pplx.app sites, enterprise users can build and internally share sites while keeping strict control over who can open them.

Visibility controls apply to Perplexity-hosted pplx.app sites. Sites deployed to Vercel are hosted and governed by Vercel, so their access is managed on Vercel.

Publishing with Vercel

Vercel is a third-party hosting platform for deploying websites and web apps. Publishing with Vercel is useful when you want to go beyond a pplx.app address — for example, to serve your site on your own custom domain, or to manage hosting and infrastructure yourself on Vercel.

A few things to know about how it works:

  • It uses the Vercel connector. Selecting Publish with Vercel submits a follow-up request that connects to Vercel and deploys your site through the connector. If your Vercel account isn’t connected yet, you’ll be prompted to connect it first.
  • It’s a connector flow, not a separate integration. There’s no special setup beyond connecting Vercel. The button simply starts the same connector-based deployment you’d use for any other Vercel action in conversation.
  • Your site is managed on Vercel. Once deployed, the site is hosted and governed by Vercel — so custom domains, environment settings, and access are handled in your Vercel account rather than through Perplexity’s visibility controls.
  • Ask Computer to manage it. Because the site is managed through the connector, you can ask Computer for the published link, check deployment details, or set a custom domain by continuing the conversation.
How to publish with Vercel
  • To publish to Vercel, select Publish with Vercel in the publish view.
  • To connect your account, follow the Vercel connector prompt if you haven’t linked Vercel yet.
  • To find your published link or set a custom domain, ask Computer to retrieve or manage your Vercel site.

See About publishing with Vercel above for more on how this option works.

Turning off public website publishing (organization admins)

Organization admins can disable public website publishing from Organization Settings > Computer. When it’s off, users can still publish pplx.app sites but can’t make them public — only private, specific people, or organization — and the Publish with Vercel option is removed from the Publish dropdown.

Frequently asked questions

What’s the difference between “Publish to Perplexity” and “Publish with Vercel”?

Publish to Perplexity hosts your site on a Perplexity pplx.app URL with built-in visibility controls, so it can stay private or organization-only even after publishing. Publish with Vercel deploys your site to Vercel through the Vercel connector, which is the path to use for custom domains and Vercel-managed infrastructure.

Can I publish a private site?

Yes. A published pplx.app site can be private to you, limited to your organization, shared with specific people, or made public. Publishing and visibility are separate — publishing a site does not make it public unless you choose that visibility level.

Can I publish a website I brought in from another source?

Publishing is available for websites within a Computer task. If you have website files from elsewhere, you can upload them into a task and ask Computer to build and publish the site to a pplx.app URL.

How do I find or manage my Vercel site later?

Ask Computer to perform an action. Because Vercel sites are managed through the connector rather than stored separately, you can request the published link, check details, or adjust settings by continuing the conversation.

Can my organization turn off web publishing?

Publishing is designed with enterprise access control in mind — organization sites are organization-only by default and enforce your chosen visibility. Organization admins can disable public sharing from Organization Settings > Computer.

  • Connectors

Using custom API credentials in Computer

When Computer needs to reach a third-party API that does not have a built-in connector, you can securely provide your own API credentials. Your keys are encrypted and stored so they are never exposed in the agent’s trajectory (the prompts and responses exchanged with third-party models) or in the task sandbox. Providing credentials within a task works on all Perplexity clients; the credentials vault surfaces for saving and managing them are available on web.

Overview

Some tools you want Computer to use will not have a built-in connector yet. Custom credentials let you bring your own API credential so Computer can authenticate to that service on your behalf — for example, to send a transactional email, pull data from an analytics API, or look up sports scores from a data provider. Computer supports the authentication methods these APIs use: tokens, custom headers, and query parameters. If you provide a username and password, Computer combines them into an Authorization bearer credential so it can authenticate to the API securely.

When a task needs credentials, Computer prompts you with a secure form. Your credential is encrypted and injected into outgoing API requests through a secure proxy, so the raw value is never exposed to the agent’s activity or the task sandbox. You choose how long a credential should stick around: just this task, saved to your personal vault for reuse, or shared with a project so your teammates can use it too. A credential you add in a task stays available for the duration of the Computer session, and you can revoke it at any time. If you save it to your personal vault, Computer attaches it with your approval — or automatically for queries that require it, if you have set up automatic approval.

Why use custom credentials?

  • Connect services without a built-in connector. Bring your own API key for a third-party tool so Computer can work with it right away.
  • Keep secrets out of the agent’s view. Credentials are encrypted and injected through a secure proxy, so the raw value never appears in the agent’s activity or sandbox.
  • Reuse keys across your tasks. Save a credential to your personal vault once and let Computer reuse it in future tasks.
  • Share access with a project. Add a credential to a project so everyone on the team can use it, without sharing the raw key.
  • Stay in control. For each saved credential, you can choose whether to let Computer use it without your approval, or to have Computer ask for your approval before using it in a task.

Note: Before you add a custom credential, check whether the service already has a built-in connector. Connectors are the preferred way to link a third-party service, and Computer will use one when it is available. Use custom credentials for services that do not have a connector yet.

The three credential scopes

Credentials come in three scopes, based on how long they last and who can use them:

  • Session (single task): Used only within the current task, and not saved for reuse.
  • Credential vault (personal): Saved to your personal Credential vault and reusable across your own tasks.
  • Project (shared): Saved to a project so everyone on the team can use it.

The sections below explain how each one works.

Session credentials (single task)

Session credentials are the simplest option: you provide a key for one task, and it is not saved.

  • You are prompted in the task. When a task needs a credential, Computer surfaces a secure form for you to enter it. It pre-fills details like the host, name, and credential type where it can.
  • It is encrypted and proxied. Your credential is encrypted and injected into outgoing API requests through a secure proxy, so the raw value is never visible to the agent’s activity or the sandbox.
  • It lasts for the whole task. Once added, the credential is available for the rest of that task, including follow-up requests.
  • It is not saved. Session credentials are not stored for reuse — start a new task and you will be prompted again. You will have the option to save the credential to your vault if you want to reuse it in the future.

User vault credentials (personal, reusable)

Save a credential to your personal vault to reuse it across your own tasks without re-entering it each time.

  • Save from the task form. When Computer prompts you for a credential, choose the option to save it to your Credential vault to reuse it across Computer tasks.
  • Or add it directly. Go to Settings > Credential vault to add and manage credentials outside of a task.
  • Approve before each reuse. By default, Computer recognizes your saved credential and asks for your explicit approval before using it. You can select Always allow to bypass explicit approval every time the credential is needed.
  • It stays private to you. User vault credentials belong to your account and are not shared with anyone else.

Project credentials (shared with a team)

Add a credential to a project so everyone working in that project can use it — useful for a shared key your team relies on.

  • Add it in the project. Project credentials are defined in the project’s sidebar, under the Credentials section.
  • Everyone in the project can use it. All members of the project can use the credential in their tasks, without seeing the raw value.
  • Writers manage it. You must be at least a writer on the project to add or revoke a project credential, following the same access rules as project connectors.
  • Approve before each use. Within a task, Computer always asks for your approval before using a project credential. You can set up Always allow to bypass this step.

Managing your credentials

  • Find your vault in Settings > Credential vault. Your personal Credential vault lives in your account settings. You can also reach it from the Connectors page — click the Add a custom connection dropdown and then click Connect your own API.
  • Add, review, or remove keys. Open the Credential vault to add a new credential, review the ones you have saved, or remove one you no longer need.
  • Manage project credentials in the project. Add or revoke a project’s credentials from that project’s connectors settings. You must be at least a writer on the project.

Limits and privacy

  • Your raw key stays hidden. Credentials are encrypted and injected through a secure proxy, so the raw value is never exposed to the agent’s activity or the task sandbox.
  • API requests only. Custom credentials are supported for API requests that Computer can route through the secure proxy. Other secret types, such as SSH keys, are not supported today.
  • Approval is required for saved credentials. Computer asks for your explicit approval before using a saved user or project credential in a task, unless you explicitly choose Always allow to bypass explicit approval every time the credential is needed.
  • Session credentials are not stored. A session credential is used only within that Computer session, can be revoked at any time, and is not saved for reuse in later sessions, unless you explicitly choose to save the credential to your Credential vault.
  • Project credentials are shared with the project. Anyone with access to a project can use its credentials in their own tasks, so only add keys you intend to share with that team.

Frequently asked questions

What is the difference between a session, user, and project credential?

A session credential is used only in the current task and is not saved. A user vault credential is saved to your personal account and reusable across your own tasks, with your approval each time. A project credential is saved to a project so everyone on that team can use it, again with approval before each use.

Should I use a connector or a custom credential?

If the service has a built-in connector, use the connector — it is the preferred way to connect a third-party service. Use a custom credential when there is no connector available for the tool you need.

Can Computer see my API key?

No. Your credential is encrypted and injected into outgoing requests through a secure proxy, so the raw value is never visible to the agent’s activity or the task sandbox.

Why is Computer asking me to approve a credential?

For saved credentials, Computer asks for your explicit approval before using a user-vault or project credential in a task. This keeps you in control of when your saved keys are used. You can configure Computer to skip approval requests for specific credentials — to do so, select Always allow within the specific credential’s settings in your Credential vault.

How do I reuse a credential in a future task?

Save it to your Credential vault when Computer prompts you, or add it directly from the vault. In later tasks, Computer will recognize it and ask you to approve its use.

Where do I find my Credential vault?

Your personal Credential vault lives in your account settings. You can also open it from the Connectors page using the option to add a custom connection or connect your own API.

Can my teammates use my keys?

Only if you add them as project credentials. User credentials are private to your account. Project credentials can be used by everyone in the project, and you must be at least a writer on the project to add or revoke them.

Can I store other secrets, like an SSH key?

Not currently. Custom credentials support API requests that route through the secure proxy. Other secret formats, such as SSH keys, are not supported today.

Is the username and password the same as my login to that service’s website?

No. When you provide a username and password, Computer uses them to build an Authorization bearer credential for authenticating to the service’s API — it is not a general-purpose login for signing in to that service’s website. Custom credentials are only used to authenticate API requests, using tokens, custom headers, or query parameters. If you are unsure which value to enter, check the API’s documentation for how it expects requests to be authenticated.
