Using Perplexity in Slack

Perplexity’s Slack integration lets you bring Computer into your Slack workspace. With Perplexity’s Slack App, you can search the web and your Slack conversations without leaving Slack, and get cited, well-formatted answers in seconds.

How It Works

  1. Chat with Computer — Start a conversation in a DM or group chat. Ask anything, just like you would a teammate.
  2. Give Computer tasks — Prompt in natural language. Computer deploys subagents to handle research, writing, data analysis, and more — automatically.
  3. Computer works — It browses, researches, creates content, and connects your tools in the background.
  4. Iterate and improve — Keep guiding Computer as it works, or kick off a new task entirely. It remembers past context across sessions.

What You Can Do

With the Slack App, you can do the following:

  • Ask any question — Get web-sourced, cited answers directly in Slack threads
  • Search your Slack workspace — Perplexity can search through messages in your DMs, private channels, and public channels you have access to
  • Get cited sources — Every answer includes numbered citations linking back to web sources or Slack messages so you can verify and explore further
  • Work in threads — Responses are posted in threads to keep your channels organized
  • Attach files — Share documents with your questions for richer context (up to 25 MB per file)

What Your Team Can Do

  • End-to-End Workflow Automation Across Apps — Computer orchestrates complete workflows by breaking down complex projects into subtasks and routing them to specialized AI models. For example, it can take a product spec from Notion, break it down into Linear tickets, and post updates in relevant Slack channels. You can ask Computer to do so on a daily basis too.
  • Multi-App Research and Content Creation — Computer conducts deep research using its integration with hundreds of apps including Gmail, Outlook, GitHub, Slack, Notion, Snowflake, and Databricks. It can research, design, write documentation, build slides, and send emails while working autonomously in the background.
  • Long-Running Project Management — Computer can run tasks for hours, managing multiple active projects simultaneously with persistent memory. It remembers past work, maintains context across sessions, and can execute scheduled tasks while you’re offline.

Who Can Use the Slack App?

The Slack App is currently available to any user with access to Perplexity Computer.

Your workspace also needs a paid Slack plan to install the Perplexity integration.

For Admins: Setting Up the Slack App

Prerequisites

To set up the Slack App, you’ll need the following:

  • A Perplexity plan with Perplexity Computer
  • Slack admin permissions to install apps in your workspace
  • A paid Slack plan

Security & Compliance

Computer is built on Perplexity’s secure infrastructure that is SOC 2 Type II certified.

  • End-to-end encryption — All data in transit and at rest is encrypted
  • No AI training on your data — Slack messages and enterprise data are never used for model training
  • User-level permissions — The Slack connector respects Slack’s native access controls. Users can only access content they’re already authorized to view.
  • Audit Logs — Admins in eligible organizations can view all submitted queries that use the Slack connector, and see every source used to generate answers, within the Audit Logs feature

Installation Steps

  1. Go to the Slack App Marketplace and search for “Perplexity Computer” from Slack’s App Marketplace. You can also find it here.
  2. Click Add to Slack
  3. Review the permissions the app requires and click Allow
  4. You’ll be redirected to a Perplexity confirmation page once installation is complete

Permissions Granted

When installing, the Perplexity app requests the following Slack permissions:

  • Permission
  • Purpose
  • app_mentions:read
  • View messages that directly mention the bot in conversations
  • channels:history
  • View messages and other content in public channels the bot has been added to
  • channels:join
  • Join public channels in a workspace
  • channels:read
  • View basic information about public channels in a workspace
  • chat:write
  • Send messages and responses in channels and DMs
  • commands
  • Add shortcuts and/or slash commands
  • files:read
  • View files shared in channels and conversations the bot has been added to
  • files:write
  • Upload, edit, and delete files as the bot
  • groups:history
  • View messages and other content in private channels the bot has been added to
  • groups:read
  • View basic information about private channels the bot has been added to
  • im:history
  • View messages and other content in direct messages the bot has been added to
  • im:read
  • View basic information about direct messages the bot has been added to
  • im:write
  • Start direct messages with people
  • metadata.message:read
  • View message metadata in channels the bot has been added to
  • mpim:history
  • View messages and other content in group direct messages the bot has been added to
  • mpim:read
  • View basic information about group direct messages the bot has been added to
  • users:read
  • View people in a workspace
  • users:read.email
  • View email addresses of people in a workspace
  • team:read
  • View the name, email domain, and icon for the workspace

Note: The bot only accesses messages on behalf of users who have authorized it. It respects Slack’s native permission model — users can only search content they already have access to.

What Your Users Will Experience

To use Computer in Slack, users can send a DM to the Slack App. Computer can also be activated by tagging 0 when writing a workflow in Slack.

Any workflows triggered in Slack will also be available on computer.

Managing the Integration

Removing the Slack App

You can uninstall the Perplexity app from your Slack workspace’s App Management page at any time. You’ll need to be a workspace owner/admin or have app management permission.

  1. On desktop, click your workspace name at the top left.
  2. Click Tools & settings (or Settings & administration) → Manage apps to open the Slack App Directory for your workspace.
  3. Under Installed apps, click the app you want to remove.
  4. On the app’s page, go to its Configuration tab and choose Remove app or Uninstall at the bottom, then confirm.
Controlling access

Use Slack’s built-in app management to control which channels the bot can be added to and which users can interact with it

Connector settings

Manage the Slack connector from your Perplexity Enterprise admin panel under your Organization Settings → Connectors. Admins can enable or disable the connector for the entire organization.

For Members: Using the Slack App

Once your admin has installed the Perplexity app in your Slack workspace, you can start using it immediately:

  1. Open a DM with Perplexity — Find “Perplexity” in your Slack apps list or direct messages and start typing.
  2. Use the Assistant shortcut — Click the vertical ellipsis (⋮) in the top-right of your Slack window, add Perplexity as a shortcut in your top bar, then click the Perplexity icon to start a conversation.
  3. Open the App Home — Navigate to the Perplexity app home in Slack for a welcome screen with quick links.

Alternatively, add Perplexity Computer to a group chat to start asking questions.

Asking Questions

To use Computer in Slack, send a message just as you would to anyone else in Slack. Perplexity will reply in the same conversation.

Examples:
  • “What were the key takeaways from our Q4 planning discussion?” (Searches Slack messages)
  • “What is the latest guidance from the SEC on AI disclosures?” (Searches the web)
  • “Summarize what our team discussed about the product launch in #general this week” (combines Slack + web context)
  • “Address the bug in AGT-2722 and spin up Codex and Opus agents to fix. Have each agent review the other’s PR. Give that agent relevant feedback and merge the best one.” (Uses the Linear + Github connectors)
  • “Identify the source of new subscriptions and which channels are bringing in the most customers.” (Uses the Snowflake connector)
  • “Find all discount codes online that violate our terms of service. Pull the data and tell me how many of them are redeemed per day.”
For Best Results
  • Be specific — The more context in your question, the better the answer
  • Use threads — Computer remembers past context across threads and sessions
  • Attach files — You can attach documents to your message for the bot to reference (up to 25 MB per file)
  • Ask about your workspace — Perplexity searches both the web and accessible Slack messages, making it great for finding past conversations and decisions
Good to Know
  • Privacy — Perplexity only accesses Slack channels and messages that you have access to. It respects Slack’s existing permission model.
  • Response length — Long answers are automatically split into multiple messages in the thread for readability
  • Status indicator — You’ll see “Answering…” while Perplexity processes your question

Frequently Asked Questions

What should I do if Computer in Slack is not responding to my messages?

If Computer in Slack isn’t responding, you may be using an outdated version of the app, particularly if you installed it before March 13, 2026. Try reinstalling the app in your Slack workspace to make sure you are using the latest version.

Does Perplexity store my Slack messages?

Perplexity accesses Slack messages in real-time to answer questions but does not permanently store your Slack data. Context is deleted after each query is processed.

Can the Slack App access private channels?

The Slack App can only search messages in channels and DMs that the requesting user has access to. It follows Slack’s native permission model.

Is there a limit to how many questions I can ask?

Slack App usage counts toward each user’s Computer limits on their Perplexity plan. You will need credits to use the Slack App.

Why do I need a paid Slack plan?

Slack requires a paid plan for third-party app installations. This is a Slack requirement, not a Perplexity one.

Can I use the Slack App and the Slack Connector at the same time?

Yes! The Slack App lets you ask questions inside Slack, while the Slack Connector lets you search Slack content from Perplexity’s web app. They complement each other.

What is Computer?

What is Computer?

Computer is an independent digital worker that completes tasks and workflows for you. Instead of just providing information, it takes action: synthesizing large amounts of information across the web and in your own work context through hundreds of app connectors. Using this rich context, it can craft assets, create functioning apps, manage emails, conduct research, and more — all in your preferred style. It can monitor and act on schedule too, so it’s always-on even when you’re off the clock.

Computer unifies every current AI capability into a single worker, including:

  • Search-native intelligence for accurate, cited information
  • Tool execution to complete tasks across platforms
  • Rich media generation for creating visual content
  • Document creation for reports and presentations
  • Authenticated integrations with Gmail, Outlook, GitHub, Linear, Slack, Notion, and more
  • Persistent memory that retains context across sessions
  • Scheduling and automation for recurring tasks
  • Secure cloud/isolated sandbox to protect your data
  • Universal access with no complicated local install

Built on Perplexity’s core strengths of accurate AI, superior deep research, advanced assistants (agents), multi-model orchestration, and safety and security, Computer represents the next evolution of AI.

How does Computer work?

Computer combines several powerful capabilities to complete complex tasks on your behalf:

Asynchronous Execution Computer runs tasks in the background while you’re away. It can monitor your email, calendar, flight status, and files with condition-based triggers, and supports scheduled jobs and proactive actions like morning briefings and deadline reminders.

Wide Research & Parallel Web Search Computer can handle queries that require researching multiple items simultaneously, making it ideal for comprehensive market research and competitive analysis.

Sub-Agents Computer uses domain-specific assistants that handle distinct task types:

  • Market research agents for competitive intelligence
  • Financial analysis agents for earnings reports and stock monitoring
  • News monitoring agents for breaking updates
  • Coordination agents that orchestrate handoffs between specialists

Premium Data Integrations Computer connects with the tools you already use, including Gmail, Outlook, GitHub, Linear, Slack, Notion, Snowflake, Databricks, and Salesforce, along with finance tools and premium data connectors.

Personal Cloud Sandbox Your data is kept in a secure, isolated environment with persistent memory across all sessions and platforms. Computer retains context from long conversations and automatically stores your preferences.

Multi-Tool Orchestration Computer coordinates multiple tools and capabilities seamlessly, retrieving and synthesizing real-time information while handling multi-step reasoning and complex task automation.

What’s the difference between Perplexity and Computer?

Perplexity answers your questions. Computer does your work.

  • Perplexity = search + knowledge. You ask, it finds and synthesizes information.
  • Computer = an agent with tools. You ask, it actually does things — writes docs, builds slides, sends emails, browses the web, writes code, connects to your apps, and chains multi-step workflows together.

One gives you answers. The other takes action.

The key differentiator: Composition

Capabilities chain together naturally. A single prompt can trigger research → analysis → document creation → email delivery → scheduled follow-up, all without you lifting a finger between steps.

Example workflow:

“Research our top 5 competitors, compare their pricing, create a slide deck with the findings, and email it to the team.”

Computer will run parallel web searches, build a comparison table, generate a branded PPTX, and send it via your connected Gmail — all in one session.

How to access Computer?

Computer is available to user with an active subscription. If you have an active subscription, you can access Computer directly within your Perplexity workspace - no additional setup or installation is required.

Computer is available on web desktop, iOS, and Android. ​ To access Computer:

  1. Go to the Home page.

  2. Click the Computer icon. ​

  3. In Computer panel, Click the Start a task field to create a new task.

​

  1. Select the Perplexity icon to switch back to Ask Anything mode and exit Computer.

​

How To Connect Apps and Service

  1. In the Computer panel, select Connectors to view available apps and services.
  2. To enable a connector, select Enable next to the app you want to connect.
  3. To install an app, select Install in the app’s tile.
  4. To disconnect, select the three dots next to the connected app, then choose Disconnect. ​

How Computer uses credits

Computer uses a credit-based system to power its advanced capabilities:

What you get:

  • Max subscribers receive 10,000 credits monthly

  • Limited-time bonus:

  • Additional 35,000 credits for paid Max subscription users

  • 4,000 credits for paid Pro subscription users

  • Your Perplexity Ask searches remain unlimited and don’t use credits

How credits work:

  • Credits are used only for Computer tasks and other premium features
  • Credit consumption varies based on task complexity

Managing your credits:

  • View your credit balance and usage at credits
  • Enable auto-refill to automatically purchase more credits when your balance drops below 500 credits
  • Set spending limits to control your monthly credit purchases
  • If your credits run out, active tasks pause (not cancel) and resume automatically when credits are available ​

For complete details about credit pricing, renewals, refund policies, and spending limits, see our Help Center article here: ../articles/13838041-how-credits-work-on-perplexity.html

Connectors & Integrations

Connecting Perplexity with Confluence

The Confluence Connector enables you to use Perplexity to search and create content on your Confluence pages. This connector is available to users in all our paid plans – Perplexity Pro, Perplexity Max, Enterprise Pro and Enterprise Max.

We’re currently rolling out the Confluence Connector to our users. If it’s not yet available to you, it will become available in the coming weeks.

Below you’ll find an overview of this Connector and how to activate it.

What does the Confluence Connector do?

The Confluence Connector allows you to seamlessly access relevant information from Confluence pages and data directly in Perplexity, and integrate it with information from other apps and services using our App Connectors, without having to shift between platforms.

With the Confluence Connector, you can also create new pages in your teamspace, and update or delete your existing pages directly from Perplexity, saving you time and context-switching.

Privacy and Data Security

The Confluence Connector requires the following permissions to function:

  • Delete pages and space information
  • View page, blogpost, comments, space and custom content and details
  • View and download attachments of a page or blogpost that you have access to
  • View database, Smart Link, folder, whiteboard data, and content templates
  • View user information in Confluence that you have access to, including usernames, email addresses and profile pictures
  • Read all content
  • Create and update pages, blogposts, comments, spaces, and space information
  • Search Confluence
  • Access to your name, email address, tasks, projects, and workspaces
  • Create and modify tasks, projects, and comments on your behalf

We do not store your Confluence data. Every time you run a query using the Confluence Connector, we retrieve only the information that is relevant to answer your query.

Enterprise-grade Security and Control

For Enterprise organizations, we offer enterprise-grade protections and compliance including SOC 2 Type II certification, end-to-end encryption, strict data privacy measures, and granular user access controls. Your Confluence data, just like any other Enterprise data, is never used for AI training, and robust safeguards ensure confidentiality and regulatory compliance. You can learn more in our Trust Center.

Connecting Perplexity to your Confluence account is done at a personal level. This means that no one else in your organization is able to query your data. If you query Confluence on a shared session, however, anyone with access to the session will be able to see the responses.

To maintain control over how Perplexity interacts with Confluence, organization admins can enable or disable the connector for all organization users.

How to activate it

To activate the Confluence Connector:

  1. Go to Connectors in Settings and locate the Confluence Connector on the list.
  2. Click Enable.
  3. Enter your Confluence base URL.
  4. You will then be prompted to log in to your Atlassian account and to grant some permissions to the connector. Click Allow to continue.
  5. Click Finish setup to start using Perplexity with Confluence.

Trying it out

Once you have connected Perplexity to Confluence, try running some of these queries to leverage your inbox and calendar information and perform actions in directly from Perplexity:

  • Search Confluence spaces for information and meeting notes related to Project X
  • Update Project X documentation pages based on new features planned
  • Search our Confluence spaces for any existing documentation on GDPR compliance procedures and supplement it with the latest EU regulatory updates from 2025
  • Combining Confluence knowledge with web research for comprehensive answers
  • Analyze our product roadmap documents in Confluence alongside current market trends for enterprise AI tools to identify potential feature opportunities
  • Review our Confluence knowledge base structure and suggest improvements based on information architecture best practices for enterprise documentation

Connecting Perplexity with Gmail and Google Calendar

The Gmail and Google Calendar Connector enables you to query email messages and calendar events directly from Perplexity.

It integrates Gmail and Google Calendar directly with Perplexity’s productivity features, streamlining your personal information management. It allows you to instantly discover and combine relevant information across your email, calendar, documents, connected apps, and the web, without exhaustive manual searches or context switching.

Below you’ll find an overview of this Connector and how to activate it.

What does the Gmail and Google Calendar Connector do?

Once enabled, the Gmail and Google Calendar Connector allows you to query your Gmail (and Google Workspace Mail) from Perplexity, enriching your searches with information that may only live in your inbox.

Currently, the Gmail and Google Calendar Connector can only query one primary calendar. Querying multiple or secondary calendars is not possible.

You can also query your calendar, allowing you to see your events or even update your calendar in a single interface.

Additionally, if you use Comet, you can create events or send emails directly from the Assistant.

Privacy and Data Security

The Gmail and Google Calendar Connector requires the following permissions to function:

  1. Contact Permissions:
  • Access to view and download contacts from “Other contacts”
  • Access to view and download your contacts
  • Access to view and download your organization’s GSuite directory
  1. Calendar Permissions:
  • View your subscribed Google calendars list
  • Add and remove Google calendar subscriptions
  • View and edit events on all your calendars
  • Access your calendar availability
  • View and download accessible calendars
  1. Email Permissions:
  • Read, compose, and send emails from your Gmail account
Enterprise-grade Security and Control

For Enterprise organizations, we offer enterprise-grade protections and compliance including SOC 2 Type II certification, end-to-end encryption, strict data privacy measures, and granular user access controls. Your emails and calendar data, just like any other Enterprise data, are never used for AI training, and robust safeguards ensure confidentiality and regulatory compliance. You can learn more in our Trust Center.

Connecting Perplexity to your Gmail and Google Calendar account is done at a personal level. This means that no one else in your organization is able to query your inbox or calendar. If you query your calendar on a shared session, however, anyone with access to the session will be able to see the responses.

To maintain control over how Perplexity interacts with Gmail and Google Calendar, organization admins can enable or disable the connector for all organization users.

How to activate it

To activate the Gmail and Google Calendar Connector:

  1. Go to Connectors in Settings and locate the Gmail with Calendar Connector on the list.
  2. Click Enable.
  3. You will then be prompted to log in to Google and to grant some permissions to the connector. These will allow the connector to have visibility of your inbox and calendar information and perform actions on your behalf in Gmail and Google Calendar. Click Allow to continue.
  4. Click Finish setup to start using Perplexity with Gmail and Google Calendar.

If you’re a member of an Enterprise organization and can’t enable the Gmail and Google Calendar Connector on your account, an admin may have disabled it for your organization. Get in touch with an organization admin who will be able to confirm this.

If it’s enabled for your organization but you still experience issues, contact us.

Trying it out

Once you have connected Perplexity to Gmail and Google Calendar, try running some of these queries to leverage your inbox and calendar information and perform actions in directly from Perplexity:

  • Summarize the emails I received yesterday and prioritize the emails that need my attention
  • Who am I meeting with this week? Write bios
  • List urgent emails from this week

If you use Comet, you can also ask the Assistant to:

  • Create 1 hour of recurring focus time in my calendar, every Wednesday at 9am
  • Send an email to Perplexity Enterprise Support to inquire about custom Data Retention

Connecting Perplexity with Jira

What does the Jira Connector do?

The Jira Connector enables Perplexity to search across Jira tasks, projects, and teams, create and update tasks and projects, and track progress, plan cycles, and manage workflows.

The Jira Connector can help save time avoid context-switching. For example, you can get summarized insights from Jira to get visibility of the progress of a specific project, or create quick updates for existing issues, all within Perplexity.

The Jira Connector also allows you to integrate Jira data with information from other apps and services using our App Connectors, without having to shift between platforms.

Privacy and Data Security

The Jira Connector requires the following permissions to function:

  • Take Jira administration actions (e.g. create projects and custom fields, view workflows, manage issue link types).
  • Create and edit project settings and create new project-level objects (e.g. versions and components).
  • View user information in Jira that the user has access to, including usernames, email addresses, and avatars.
  • Read Jira project and issue data, search for issues, and objects associated with issues like attachments and worklogs.

We do not store your Jira data. Every time you run a query using the Jira Connector, we retrieve only the information that is relevant to answer your query.

Additionally, when you connect Perplexity to Jira, you can only see and modify data that you are authorized to access.

Enterprise-grade Security and Control

For Enterprise organizations, we offer enterprise-grade protections and compliance including SOC 2 Type II certification, end-to-end encryption, strict data privacy measures, and granular user access controls. Your Jira data, just like any other Enterprise data, is never used for AI training, and robust safeguards ensure confidentiality and regulatory compliance. You can learn more in our Trust Center.

Connecting Perplexity to your Jira account is done at a personal level. This means that no one else in your organization is able to query your data. If you query Jira on a shared session, however, anyone with access to the session will be able to see the responses.

To maintain control over how Perplexity interacts with Jira, organization admins can enable or disable the connector for all organization users.

How to activate it

Connecting Perplexity with Linear

The Linear Connector is a new feature available to Enterprise Pro and Enterprise Max users that integrates Perplexity with Linear, allowing you to use data from Linear in your queries, enabling you to make more informed decisions, faster.

It allows you to use information from Linear and combine it with data from across your organization’s documents, connected apps, and the web, saving your team time and context-switching.

Below you’ll find an overview of this Connector and how to activate it.

How does the Linear Connector work?

The Linear Connector for Perplexity facilitates the flow of information between Linear and Perplexity, adapting to your organization’s workflows.

Users can query information from Linear issues, teams, initiatives, projects, and more, summarize and extract insights, and combine them with with information from the web or other apps contextually richer responses.

The Linear Connector also allows to create and update issues directly from Perplexity, allowing teams to move from research to synthesis to execution in seconds.

To maintain control over how Perplexity interacts with Linear, organization admins can enable or disable the connector for all organization users.

The Linear Connector keeps Perplexity in sync with Linear in real time, always searching Linear for the latest data when Lineatr is chosen as a source. Only the specific results needed to answer each query are retrieved. Perplexity does not store or index the entire Linear workspace.

Privacy and Data Security

The Linear Connector respects Linear’s user permissions, so only data that a user can access in Linear can be accessed by that user through Perplexity.

We offer enterprise-grade protections and compliance including SOC 2 Type II certification, end-to-end encryption, strict data privacy measures, and granular user access controls. Your emails and calendar data, just like any other Enterprise data, are never used for AI training, and robust safeguards ensure confidentiality and regulatory compliance. You can learn more in our Trust Center.

Additionally, admins in eligible organizations can view all submitted queries that use the Linear Connector, and see every source used to generate answers, within Audit Logs. However, answers themselves and the specific Linear tickets accessed are not currently tracked in Audit Logs.

How to activate it

To activate the Linear Connector:

  1. Go to Connectors in Settings and locate the Linear Connector on the list.
  2. Click Enable.
  3. You will then be prompted to log in to Linear if you haven’t done so.
  4. Click Finish setup to start using Perplexity with Linear.

If you can’t enable the Linear Connector on your account, an admin may have disabled it for your organization. Get in touch with an organization admin who will be able to confirm this.

If it’s enabled for your organization but you still experience issues, contact us.

Trying it out

Once you have connected Perplexity to Linear, ensure that you select it as a source when running a query from a session:

  1. Click + button in the compose box > Connectors and sources
  2. Select Linear and any other source you’d like to use.

Try running some of these queries to leverage your Linear information directly in your answers:

  • Show me all teams in our workspace
  • How do other companies tend to address the issues described in ticket
  • Find urgent issues created by me in the last month
  • Show me complete details for issue
  • Create a feature request issue for team collaboration

Details on our use of Merge API, Inc. as data sub-processor

We use Merge to connect your Linear instance with Perplexity and handle user authentication. While queries and app data pass through the Merge-provided connector, the data is anonymized and neither stored nor logged by Merge.

Connecting Perplexity with Notion

The Notion Connector lets Perplexity Max, Enterprise Pro, and Enterprise Max subscribers search, summarize, and combine insights from their Notion pages and databases with information from other connected apps and organization documents in a single workflow.

You can combine your Notion data with information from across your organization’s documents, connected apps, and the web, making you more productive, and saving you time and context-switching.

How does the Notion Connector work?

The Notion Connector for Perplexity brings together your Notion and Perplexity workspaces, allowing you to enrich your answers with information from different sources.

For example, you can query information from Notion pages or databases, summarize and extract insights, and combine these with information from the web or other apps contextually richer responses.

The Notion Connector keeps Perplexity in sync with Notion in real time, always searching Notion for the latest data when Notion is chosen as a source. Only the specific results needed to answer each query are retrieved. Perplexity does not store or index the entire Notion workspace.

Privacy and Data Security

The Notion Connector requires the following permissions to function:

  • Mirror your page and database access and permissions
  • Take actions on your behalf, based on your access level
  • Search across connected apps (requires Notion AI plan)
  • View workspace’s users and their emails

The Notion Connector respects Notion’s user permissions, so only data that a user can access in Notion can be accessed by that user through Perplexity.

We offer enterprise-grade protections and compliance including SOC 2 Type II certification, end-to-end encryption, strict data privacy measures, and granular user access controls. Your emails and calendar data, just like any other Enterprise data, are never used for AI training, and robust safeguards ensure confidentiality and regulatory compliance. You can learn more in our Trust Center.

Additionally, admins in eligible organizations can view all submitted queries that use the Notion Connector, and see every source used to generate answers, within Audit Logs. However, answers themselves and the specific Notion documents accessed are not currently tracked in Audit Logs.

How to activate it

To activate the Notion Connector:

  1. Go to Connectors in Settings and locate the Notion Connector on the list.
  2. Click Enable.
  3. You will then be prompted to log in to Notion and to grant some permissions to the connector. These will allow the connector to read information and perform actions on your behalf in the Notion workspace. Click Allow to continue.
  4. Click Finish setup to start using Perplexity with Notion.

In Enterprise organizations, organization admins can enable or disable the connector for all organization users.

If you can’t enable the Notion Connector on your account, get in touch with an organization admin.

Trying it out

Once you have connected Perplexity to Notion, ensure that you select it as a source when running a query from a session:

  1. Click Set sources for search
  2. Select Notion and any other source you’d like to use.

Connecting Perplexity with Outlook.com

The Outlook Connector enables you to query Outlook.com and Outlook on the Web email messages and calendar events directly from Perplexity.

It integrates Outlook.com or Outlook on the Web directly with Perplexity’s productivity features, streamlining your personal information management. You can seamlessly access and integrate relevant information from your email, calendar, documents, connected applications, and the web—all without having to search manually or shift between platforms.

Below you’ll find an overview of this Connector and how to activate it.

What does the Outlook.com Connector do?

Once enabled, the Outlook.com Connector allows you to query your Outlook.com or Outlook on the Web inbox from Perplexity, enriching your searches with information that you may only have in your emails.

Currently, the Outlook.com Connector can only query one primary calendar. Querying multiple or secondary calendars is not possible.

You can also query your Outlook calendar, allowing you to see your events in a single interface.

In the future, you will be able to send emails or create calendar events directly from Perplexity and Comet.

Privacy and Data Security

The Outlook.com Connector requires the following permissions to function:

  • Maintain access to data you have given it access to
  • Sign you in and read your profile
  • Read your mail
  • Read and write access to your mail
  • Read and write mail you can access
  • Send mail as you
  • Send mail on behalf of others or yourself
  • Have full access of your contacts
  • Have full access to your calendars
Enterprise-grade Security and Control

For Enterprise organizations, we offer enterprise-grade protections and compliance including SOC 2 Type II certification, end-to-end encryption, strict data privacy measures, and granular user access controls. Your emails and calendar data, just like any other Enterprise data, are never used for AI training, and robust safeguards ensure confidentiality and regulatory compliance. You can learn more in our Trust Center.

Connecting Perplexity to your Outlook.com account is done at a personal level. This means that no one else in your organization is able to query your inbox or calendar. If you query your calendar on a shared session, however, anyone with access to the session will be able to see the responses.

To maintain control over how Perplexity interacts with Outlook.com, organization admins can enable or disable the connector for all organization users.

How to activate it

To activate the Outlook.com Connector:

  1. Go to Connectors in Settings and locate the Outlook.com on the list.
  2. Click Enable.
  3. You will then be prompted to log in to your Microsoft account and to grant some permissions to the connector. These will allow the connector to have visibility of your inbox and calendar information and perform actions on your behalf in Outlook.com or Outlook on the Web. Click Allow to continue.
  4. Click Finish setup to start using Perplexity with Outlook.

If you’re a member of an Enterprise organization, admin approval may be required to allow Perplexity to connect to your organization’s Outlook account. Your organization admin can follow these instructions to grant this approval.

Trying it out

Once you have connected Perplexity to Outlook.com, try running some of these queries to leverage your inbox and calendar information and perform actions in directly from Perplexity:

  • Summarize the emails I received yesterday and prioritize the emails that need my attention
  • Who am I meeting with this week? Write bios
  • List urgent emails from this week

Connecting Perplexity with Snowflake

About the Snowflake Connector

The Snowflake Connector lets you query data in your Snowflake data warehouse directly from Perplexity. You can search and combine information across your Snowflake databases, your other connected apps, and the web — without writing SQL by hand or switching to the Snowflake console.

It is available on Perplexity Pro, Perplexity Max, Enterprise Pro, and Enterprise Max. Connecting Snowflake is done per user — no one else in your organization can query your data unless you sync it to a shared Project.

This guide covers how the connector works, how to choose an authentication method, how to configure roles and read-only access, and a step-by-step setup. If you just want the fastest path: most organizations should use User OAuth — jump to Setup guide.

What the connector can access

Once enabled, the connector securely connects to your Snowflake account and lets you search across the databases, schemas, tables, and views you are authorized to see. When your Snowflake data changes, the connector reflects those changes automatically on your next query.

Supported data objects:

  • Tables
  • Views and materialized views
  • Schemas
  • Databases
  • Structured data (CSV-, JSON-, and Parquet-backed tables)

Unstructured data (images, audio, and video stored in Snowflake stages) is not supported.

Choosing an authentication method

Snowflake authentication has two independent dimensions: who the queries run as (the identity) and how that identity proves itself (the credential). Perplexity ships two connectors in the directory, each matched to a recommended combination:

  • Snowflake (User OAuth) — each Perplexity user signs in with their own Snowflake account (0) via OAuth. Recommended for most organizations.
  • Snowflake — all Perplexity users query through a single shared service account (0) using a key pair. Use this when your end users do not have individual Snowflake accounts.
Recommended: User OAuth

With User OAuth, each person authenticates with their own credentials, queries run under their native Snowflake permissions, and you get a clear per-user audit trail — no shared service account and no need to recreate your role-based access control (RBAC) inside Perplexity. This also matches Snowflake’s own direction: its managed MCP server only supports OAuth 2.0 today.

Note: With OAuth, the access token is bound to a single primary role at sign-in — the user’s 0. To make sure users can reach everything they have been granted, configure secondary roles (see User OAuth role configuration).

Fallback: service account (key pair)

A shared service account (0 + key pair) is appropriate when:

  • Your end users do not have individual Snowflake accounts (for example, business users who query through Perplexity but have no direct Snowflake access).
  • You want all Perplexity queries to run under a single shared identity for simpler auditing.
  • You are setting up an internal or test environment where per-user identity is not required.

Configure the service account with a dedicated, narrowly-scoped role — see Service account role configuration.

A note on Programmatic Access Tokens (PATs): Snowflake also supports PAT credentials, and the connector accepts them. We do not recommend PAT for new setups — tokens expire on a short schedule and are hard-bound to a single role at creation time, so we use a key pair in the examples throughout this guide. If you must use a PAT, set 0 to your dedicated Perplexity role at creation time.

Configuring roles and access

User OAuth role configuration

An OAuth session opens with the user’s 0 as the primary role; role switching within a session is not supported and Perplexity does not show a role picker. So whatever each user has set for 1 and 2 is exactly what their Perplexity session uses.

Set this once, at the security integration level:

OAUTH_USE_SECONDARY_ROLES = IMPLICIT

Snowflake’s default for this parameter is NONE, which means OAuth sessions open with only the primary role active. Setting it to 0 tells Snowflake to also activate each user’s 1 automatically. Without it, users see only data reachable through their 2 — rarely what you want.

Then handle one of two cases:

Case A — users already have role defaults. If your users’ 0 and 1 are already set the way you want, do nothing else. The 2 setting is enough — each user’s existing defaults are what their Perplexity session uses.

Case B — users do not yet have role defaults. Give each session the union of all roles granted to that user by setting 0 to 1 and 2 to 3:

ALTER USER <username> SET DEFAULT_ROLE = PUBLIC, DEFAULT_SECONDARY_ROLES = ('ALL');

Note: Since Snowflake’s August 2024 change, 0 is the default for newly created users, so many orgs already have this set. Run 1 to check before changing anything.

Service account role configuration

A service account is a single dedicated user that runs under one specific role — typically 0. The 1 + 2 pattern used for OAuth does not apply here. Instead, set the service user’s 3 to your dedicated role and grant that role only the privileges Perplexity needs. This follows Snowflake’s service account best practices.

The full SQL for creating this user is in the Setup guide below.

(Optional) Restricting which roles can be used via OAuth

To limit which Snowflake roles users can authenticate into through the Perplexity OAuth integration, scope it at the security integration level:

  • 0 — an allowlist of roles permitted through this integration.
  • 0 — a denylist of roles blocked from this integration.
ALTER SECURITY INTEGRATION PERPLEXITY_OAUTH SET PRE_AUTHORIZED_ROLES_LIST = ('ANALYST_ROLE', 'READ_ONLY_ROLE');

Use this to keep sensitive roles (for example 0) out of the OAuth flow entirely.

Controlling what Perplexity can do

The connector executes SQL through a tool surface (sourced from the Merge Snowflake connector). The Snowflake (User OAuth) connector exposes granular, named tools — including a dedicated read-only query tool (0) that only accepts 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, and 7, and rejects DDL/DML and multi-statement input. The service-account Snowflake connector exposes a general 8 tool that accepts arbitrary SQL, including DDL and DML.

The authoritative control is Snowflake RBAC. No matter which tool is invoked, queries run under the role your configuration allows, so enforcing read-only at the Snowflake role level is the reliable backstop — and it is the only enforcement boundary on the service-account connector.

Recommended pattern — enforce read-only with a dedicated role. Provision a role such as 0 that grants only 1 and 2 on the databases, schemas, and tables you want Perplexity to query, and does not grant 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8, or warehouse 9. Then make sure users authenticate into that role:

  • Set it as the user’s 0, or
  • Scope the OAuth integration with 0 and/or exclude write-capable roles with 1 (see Restricting which roles can be used via OAuth).

With RBAC configured this way, Snowflake rejects any write Perplexity attempts, regardless of which tool is called.

Note: The connector settings include a Tool permissions panel listing available tools. On the Snowflake (User OAuth) connector you can narrow which tools are exposed — for a read-only setup, enable only the read tools (for example 0, 1, 2, 3) to keep write/DDL tools off the surface. Treat this as defense-in-depth, not the authoritative boundary: Snowflake RBAC (above) is what reliably enforces access.

Privacy and data security

When connected, the Snowflake Connector can perform the following actions on your behalf:

  • Execute SQL queries on your Snowflake data
  • Query Cortex Search and Cortex Analyst
  • Run custom UDFs and stored procedures

If you revoke access or remove a role in Snowflake, that data is immediately inaccessible from Perplexity. If you disconnect Snowflake in Perplexity, you can choose whether to keep or delete any cached data.

Enterprise-grade security and control

For Enterprise organizations, Perplexity offers SOC 2 Type II certification, end-to-end encryption, strict data privacy measures, and granular user access controls. Your Snowflake data is never used for AI training.

Connecting Snowflake is done per user — no one else in your organization can query your data. However, if you sync data to a shared Project, anyone with access to that Project can search it.

Organization admins can enable or disable the connector for all users from the Permissions screen in Organization Settings. Read/write enforcement is handled at the Snowflake role level (see Controlling what Perplexity can do).

Setup guide

Choose the path that matches your authentication method:

  • User OAuth (recommended): An org admin configures the OAuth app once, then users sign in individually. Skip straight to Connect Snowflake to Perplexity — the SQL in steps 1–6 is for service-account setups only.
  • Service account (key pair): Run the SQL setup in steps 1–6, then connect in step 7.
Prerequisites
  • 0 role (or a role with 1 and 2 privileges)
  • OpenSSL installed locally (pre-installed on macOS) — service-account setups only
Step 1: Generate a key pair (service account)

From your terminal, generate an RSA private key:

openssl genrsa 2048 | openssl pkcs8 -topk8 -inform PEM -out snowflake_computer_key.p8 -nocrypt

Then generate the matching public key:

openssl rsa -in snowflake_computer_key.p8 -pubout -out snowflake_computer_key.pub
Step 2: Create a role and warehouse
USE ROLE ACCOUNTADMIN;-- Create a dedicated roleCREATE ROLE IF NOT EXISTS PERPLEXITY_ROLE COMMENT = 'Role for Perplexity service account';-- Create a warehouse (or use an existing one)CREATE WAREHOUSE IF NOT EXISTS PERPLEXITY_WAREHOUSE WAREHOUSE_SIZE = 'XSMALL' AUTO_SUSPEND = 60 AUTO_RESUME = TRUE COMMENT = 'Warehouse for Perplexity queries';-- Grant warehouse usage to the roleGRANT USAGE ON WAREHOUSE PERPLEXITY_WAREHOUSE TO ROLE PERPLEXITY_ROLE;
Step 3: Create the service account user

Run this as 0. Replace the 1 value with the contents of your 2 file (without the header and footer lines).

USE ROLE ACCOUNTADMIN;CREATE USER PERPLEXITY_USER TYPE = SERVICE DEFAULT_ROLE = PERPLEXITY_ROLE DEFAULT_WAREHOUSE = PERPLEXITY_WAREHOUSE COMMENT = 'Service account for Perplexity' RSA_PUBLIC_KEY = 'MIIBIjANBgkqhki...your_public_key_here...IDAQAB';-- Grant the dedicated role to the service accountGRANT ROLE PERPLEXITY_ROLE TO USER PERPLEXITY_USER;

Note: 0 marks this as a non-human account — do not set a password. 1 scopes it to one dedicated role with only the privileges Perplexity needs.

Step 4: Grant access to your data
-- Grant access to a specific databaseGRANT USAGE ON DATABASE MY_DATABASE TO ROLE PERPLEXITY_ROLE;-- Grant access to all schemas in a database (current and future)GRANT USAGE ON ALL SCHEMAS IN DATABASE MY_DATABASE TO ROLE PERPLEXITY_ROLE;GRANT USAGE ON FUTURE SCHEMAS IN DATABASE MY_DATABASE TO ROLE PERPLEXITY_ROLE;-- Grant read access to all tables and views (current and future)GRANT SELECT ON ALL TABLES IN DATABASE MY_DATABASE TO ROLE PERPLEXITY_ROLE;GRANT SELECT ON FUTURE TABLES IN DATABASE MY_DATABASE TO ROLE PERPLEXITY_ROLE;GRANT SELECT ON ALL VIEWS IN DATABASE MY_DATABASE TO ROLE PERPLEXITY_ROLE;GRANT SELECT ON FUTURE VIEWS IN DATABASE MY_DATABASE TO ROLE PERPLEXITY_ROLE;

For more selective access, see the Snowflake GRANT documentation.

Step 5: Grant access to query history and access history

Perplexity uses two views in the 0 schema to build a Data Map for accurate query generation:

  • View
  • Purpose
  • QUERY_HISTORY
  • Query metadata, performance, and usage patterns
  • ACCESS_HISTORY
  • Object-level audit trail (Enterprise Edition only)
USE ROLE ACCOUNTADMIN;GRANT IMPORTED PRIVILEGES ON DATABASE SNOWFLAKE TO ROLE PERPLEXITY_ROLE;

Note: 0 is only available on Snowflake Enterprise Edition or higher; Perplexity falls back to 1 on Standard Edition automatically. Skipping this step reduces query accuracy. For how Perplexity uses these views, see Understanding the Data Map.

Verify access
USE ROLE ACCOUNTADMIN;GRANT ROLE PERPLEXITY_ROLE TO USER <your_username>;USE ROLE PERPLEXITY_ROLE;USE WAREHOUSE PERPLEXITY_WAREHOUSE;SELECT * FROM SNOWFLAKE.ACCOUNT_USAGE.QUERY_HISTORY LIMIT 5;SELECT * FROM SNOWFLAKE.ACCOUNT_USAGE.ACCESS_HISTORY LIMIT 5;
Step 6: (Optional) Allowlist IP addresses

If your Snowflake account restricts inbound connections by IP, allowlist the IPs from this page:

USE ROLE ACCOUNTADMIN;CREATE NETWORK POLICY PERPLEXITY_COMPUTER_POLICY ALLOWED_IP_LIST = (...US IPs from the link above...);ALTER USER PERPLEXITY_USER SET NETWORK_POLICY = PERPLEXITY_COMPUTER_POLICY;
Step 7: Connect Snowflake to Perplexity
For User OAuth: admin setup first

If your organization uses OAuth, an org admin configures the Snowflake (User OAuth) connector once before individual users can authenticate. In Organization Settings → Connectors → Snowflake (User OAuth), the admin sees a three-step pane:

  1. Manage OAuth app — register Perplexity as an OAuth client against your Snowflake account. This walks you through creating a custom Snowflake 0 and pasting the resulting Client ID and Client Secret back into Perplexity.
  2. Authenticate with Snowflake (User OAuth) — the admin authenticates once to verify the integration works end-to-end.
  3. Generate a data map (optional) — optionally generate a data map for the organization (see Understanding the Data Map) and add supplementary context to improve accuracy.

In step 1, create the security integration on the Snowflake side. Run this as 0:

USE ROLE ACCOUNTADMIN;CREATE SECURITY INTEGRATION PERPLEXITY_OAUTH TYPE = OAUTH ENABLED = TRUE OAUTH_CLIENT = CUSTOM OAUTH_CLIENT_TYPE = 'CONFIDENTIAL' OAUTH_REDIRECT_URI = 'https://ah.merge.dev/oauth/callback' OAUTH_ISSUE_REFRESH_TOKENS = TRUE OAUTH_REFRESH_TOKEN_VALIDITY = 7776000 OAUTH_USE_SECONDARY_ROLES = IMPLICIT COMMENT = 'OAuth security integration for Perplexity';

Retrieve the Client ID and Client Secret to paste back into Perplexity:

SELECT SYSTEM$SHOW_OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRETS('PERPLEXITY_OAUTH');

Once admin setup is complete, individual users can connect using the steps below.

Connect (all users)

Users only see the auth method matching the connector their admin configured.

  1. Go to Connectors in Settings and find the Snowflake or Snowflake (User OAuth) connector.
  2. Click Enable → Add Connector.
  3. Authenticate using your configured method (see below).
  4. Click Allow to complete setup.

Key-pair authentication (Snowflake connector). Enter your Snowflake account identifier, username, and private key (0).

OAuth (Snowflake (User OAuth) connector). You will be redirected to Snowflake to sign in. There is no role picker — your session uses your 0 as the primary role, with your 1 activated via 2. If your role defaults are already configured, no action is needed.

If your session is missing data you expect to see, ask your Snowflake admin to verify your 0 and 1 (see User OAuth role configuration).

Using your Snowflake data

Once connected, reference your Snowflake data in Computer tasks. Mention databases, schemas, or tables and Perplexity will query them as part of multi-step workflows — all asynchronously in a secure cloud sandbox. Try queries like:

  • “Summarize the revenue trends from the Q4 sales table and highlight key metrics”
  • “Find all customer records updated in the last 30 days”
  • “What are the top-performing products based on the analytics schema?”
  • “Compare this quarter’s pipeline data against the previous quarter’s figures”

Troubleshooting

Snowflake is not enabled for your organization

If you’re in an Enterprise organization and can’t enable the connector, an admin may have disabled it. Contact your organization admin, or reach out to Perplexity support.

Connection and authentication issues
  • Verify Perplexity’s IP addresses are allowlisted in your Snowflake network policy.
  • Confirm the role has 0 privileges on the required warehouses, databases, and schemas.
  • Ask users to reconnect the connector after any policy changes.
“Invalid private key” error

Make sure you’re pasting the private key (0), not the public key. The file should start with 1.

Authentication rejected by current authentication policy

Run 0 as 1 and ensure 2 is covered by a policy that includes 3. If needed:

CREATE AUTHENTICATION POLICY PERPLEXITY_AUTH_POLICY AUTHENTICATION_METHODS = ('KEYPAIR') CLIENT_TYPES = ('DRIVERS');ALTER USER PERPLEXITY_USER SET AUTHENTICATION POLICY PERPLEXITY_AUTH_POLICY;
OAuth: user sees limited data after connecting

The OAuth session uses the user’s 0 as the primary role, with secondary roles activated via 1. If a user is missing expected access:

  • Confirm the security integration has 0.
  • Run 0 and check 1 and 2 — the session uses exactly what is configured there.
  • If the user has no per-user role configuration and you want them to access everything granted to them, set 0 and 1.
  • Have them disconnect and reconnect the connector to issue a fresh token after changing any of these settings.
ACCESS_HISTORY view returns an error

Confirm your Snowflake account is Enterprise Edition or higher.

Insufficient privileges on ACCOUNT_USAGE views

Verify 0 was run as 1 and that 2 is granted to the user.

Key-pair authentication fails

Re-run 0 to confirm the public key fingerprint is populated.

If issues persist after updating these settings, contact Perplexity support for assistance.

File App Connectors for Perplexity Pro subscribers

Perplexity Connectors: Integrating Apps and Cloud Storage with AI Search

Perplexity Connectors allow you to seamlessly integrate with File App Connectors like Google Drive with Perplexity’s AI search capabilities, enabling you to find information across your personal files. Here’s everything you need to know about this powerful feature.

What Are Perplexity Connectors?

Connectors are integrations that allow you to connect your cloud storage services to Perplexity. For Pro subscribers the following integrations are supported:

  • Google Drive
  • Dropbox

We’re working on expanding integrations with other cloud storage services, too. Setting up a Connector enables Perplexity to search through your files to provide answers based on your content.

How Connectors Work

When you connect your cloud storage service to Perplexity you will be able to easily select specific files to sync with Perplexity.

Here’s what connection with your file app would look like:

At the moment, file syncing is not supported on the Pro plan. If you make changes in the original file, they will not transfer over to the file you have attached your session. You will have to re-upload the specific file to search the latest information.

Getting Started with Connectors

To connect your cloud storage service to Perplexity:

  1. Visit your Settings page and find My Connectors in the sidebar

  2. Enable the connectors you’d like to add your Perplexity account.

  3. You will have to authenticate your account.

  4. A successful authentication will say Connected

  5. To edit files, click on the (…) three dots and then Upload.

Supported File Types

Perplexity Connectors currently support:

  • Google Docs, Slides, and Sheets
  • PDF files
  • Microsoft Office files (DOCX, XLSX, PPTX)
  • CSV files
  • Markdown (MD)
  • JSON files

Using Connectors in Your Searches

Here’s how you can use files in your Searches

  1. As a source for search: Enable your connected file app as a source when creating a new thread.
  2. As file attachments: Add specific files from your connected storage as attachments to your queries.

Please note that file connectors can only be utilized directly in a session. File connectors for projects is an Enterprise feature.

Security and Privacy

The following measures are in place to protect your data:

  • All credentials are encrypted at rest and in transit
  • Files are stored securely within AWS S3
  • Your data will not be used to train models

Troubleshooting for File App Connectors (Pro subscribers)

Google Drive Issues

Google-specific settings may block integration. Here’s how to fix them:

OAuth Scope Settings

If your organization can’t set access to “Unrestricted,” try these alternatives:

  1. Select “Restricted: Only trusted apps can access a service”

  2. Enable “For apps that are not trusted, allow users to grant access to OAuth scopes that aren’t classified as high-risk”

Drive SDK Configuration

Check your Google Workspace settings:

  1. Go to Google Workspace > Drive and Docs > Features and Applications > Drive SDK
  2. Make sure to select “Allow users to access Google Drive with the Drive SDK API”

This setting is crucial as it allows Perplexity to work with your Google Drive files.

Using the Connector for HubSpot

The HubSpot connector for Perplexity integrates your HubSpot CRM with Perplexity, letting you search and retrieve information about your contacts, companies, deals, tickets, and other CRM records directly within a Perplexity session. Ask questions about your CRM data in natural language and get instant, structured answers without leaving Perplexity.

What does the HubSpot connector do?

The HubSpot connector gives Perplexity read-only access to your HubSpot CRM data. Once connected, you can ask natural-language questions and Perplexity will query your HubSpot account to retrieve the relevant records.

Supported CRM objects include:

  • Contacts — search and list people in your CRM
  • Companies — look up companies, domains, and associated details
  • Deals — check deal stages, amounts, and pipeline status
  • Tickets — review support tickets and their statuses
  • Products, Line Items, Quotes, and Invoices — query commerce-related records

Perplexity can read and search these records but cannot create, update, or delete any data in your HubSpot account. The connector is strictly read-only.

Who can use it?

The HubSpot connector is available to Perplexity Pro, Perplexity Max, Enterprise Pro and Enterprise Max users.

You’ll need a HubSpot account with access to the CRM objects you want to query.

Privacy and Data Security

Information Perplexity can view

When you connect HubSpot, Perplexity can view CRM records you have access to in your HubSpot portal, including:

  • Contact names, emails, phone numbers, job titles, and associated properties
  • Company names, domains, industry, and other company properties
  • Deal names, stages, amounts, close dates, and pipeline information
  • Ticket subjects, statuses, priorities, and descriptions
  • Other CRM object data (products, quotes, invoices, line items)
Information Perplexity cannot access
  • Perplexity cannot create, modify, or delete any records in your HubSpot account
  • Perplexity does not store your CRM data beyond what is needed to answer your query
  • Your HubSpot credentials are managed securely through OAuth and are never exposed to Perplexity directly
Disconnecting

You can disconnect the HubSpot connector at any time from your Perplexity settings. Disconnecting immediately revokes Perplexity’s access to your HubSpot data.

How to activate the HubSpot connector

  1. Start a new conversation on 0.
  2. Click the “+” button in the query bar to open the source selector.
  3. Search for or select HubSpot from the list of available connectors.
  4. You will be redirected to HubSpot to authorize the connection. Sign in to your HubSpot account and grant Perplexity access.
  5. Once authorized, you will be returned to Perplexity. The HubSpot chip will appear in your query bar, confirming the connector is active.

Note: You need to select HubSpot or type 0 to select the connector with your query.

Note: If your HubSpot account requires admin approval for third-party app installations, you may need to contact your HubSpot admin before connecting.

Trying it out

Once connected, you can ask Perplexity questions about your HubSpot CRM data. Here are some examples to get started:

Look up your contacts

Select the HubSpot connector and type a query like:

“Who are my contacts on HubSpot?”

Perplexity will retrieve your contacts from HubSpot and display them with names, email addresses, companies, and job titles.

Browse your companies

Try asking:

“What are the companies in my HubSpot profile?”

Perplexity will return a structured table of companies in your account, including names, domains, websites, and direct links back to each record in HubSpot.

More example queries

  • “Show me all open deals in my HubSpot pipeline”
  • “What tickets are currently open in HubSpot?”
  • “Who are the contacts at ACME Corp?”
  • “What is the status of the deal with [company name]?”
  • “List my HubSpot contacts sorted by last modified date”

How it works

When you submit a query with the HubSpot connector active, Perplexity takes multiple steps to answer your question:

  1. Interprets your question and determines which HubSpot CRM objects to query.
  2. Retrieves the relevant records from your HubSpot account (you may see progress indicators like “Retrieving the user’s contacts from HubSpot” or “Searching for companies in the user’s HubSpot profile using CRM object search”).
  3. Formats the results into a clear, readable answer — often as a bulleted list or table with links back to the original records in HubSpot.

Follow-up questions

After an initial query, the HubSpot connector stays active in the conversation thread. You can ask follow-up questions to drill deeper into your CRM data without re-connecting:

  • “Tell me more about Maria Johnson”
  • “Which of these companies are in San Francisco?”
  • “What deals are associated with ACME Corp?”

Frequently Asked Questions

Can Perplexity modify my HubSpot data?

No. The HubSpot connector is read-only. Perplexity can search and retrieve your CRM records but cannot create, update, or delete any data.

What HubSpot plans are supported?

The connector works with all HubSpot plans that provide CRM API access, including HubSpot Free, Starter, Professional, and Enterprise.

Why can’t I see certain records?

Perplexity can only access records that your HubSpot user account has permission to view. If you have restricted permissions in HubSpot, those same restrictions apply when using the connector.

Is my data shared with other users?

No. The connector uses your personal HubSpot authentication. Only you can see your HubSpot data through Perplexity, and it is not shared with other Perplexity users or your team members unless they connect their own accounts.

Desktop App

About the App

The app brings Perplexity to Windows and Mac. In addition to the functionality available on the web, it can work with files and folders on your computer, use supported desktop apps and browsers, and make that device available for Remote Access.

What can the app do?

  • Find and organize local files. Ask Perplexity to find, sort, move, rename, group, or clean up files within the locations you make available.
  • Read, create, and edit files. Work with documents, spreadsheets, code, reports, and other supported files, then save the result to your computer.
  • Use a folder for related work. Choose the folder containing the files you want Perplexity to use. Within that folder, Perplexity can use files without requesting access every time. Sensitive actions may still require approval.
  • Open Perplexity from anywhere on your desktop. Use the floating task bar without leaving the app you are currently using.
  • Work with browsers and apps. Use Comet and supported installed apps on Mac. Installed-app control is not yet available on Windows.
  • Access desktop work from another device. Remote Access lets you start, continue, or monitor work on your desktop from another desktop, mobile, or the web.

How is it different from the web experience?

The web experience works with information you upload or connect. The app can also work with local resources on your computer.

You decide which folders Perplexity can use and which desktop capabilities to enable. A folder on your computer is separate from a Perplexity Project: the folder provides local files, while a Project organizes work inside Perplexity.

Where is it available?

The app is available for Windows and Mac. See Get started for installation, updates, and initial setup.

If you already have an older version, see Upgrade from the old app.

Add Files to a Request and Find Generated Files

Attaching a file is different from giving the app access to a folder.

Attach a file

Use the attachment control when you want Perplexity to read or analyze a copy of a file in the current request. Attaching a file does not make the original folder available to the app, and changes to the attached copy do not automatically update the original.

To edit the original file or save changes back into its location, select the containing folder instead of attaching a copy. Give the app access to a folder when you want Perplexity to work across several local files, edit the originals, or save new files back to that folder.

Find a generated file

When asking Perplexity to create a file, specify where it should be saved. For example:

“Create a Word document and save it to my Desktop.”

Files that Perplexity generates are always available in Artifacts. You can also open the completed task to find the same files alongside the rest of the task result.

If a file cannot be attached or downloaded

  • Try attaching it from the request field in the main app.
  • Check whether the file type and size are supported.
  • Confirm that the task finished creating the result.
  • If the task saved the file locally, check the destination named in the request.

Find Projects, Threads, and Previous Work

Find recent work

All of your sessions — Ask threads and Computer tasks — appear together in the app’s sidebar; there are no separate tabs for each. Open History from the sidebar to browse them.

Search your threads

To find a specific saved session, use search mode:

  • Mac → a dedicated input field in the side bar
  • Windows → a magnifying glass icon in the top left corner

Find a Project

A Perplexity Project is different from a local folder:

  • A Project organizes Perplexity threads, instructions, and cloud files.
  • A folder contains local files on a specific computer.

If something is missing

  • Check whether you are looking for an Ask thread, Computer task, Project, or local folder.
  • Check History in the sidebar, or the appropriate task or Project view.
  • Compare the work visible in the app with Perplexity on the web.
  • Reinstalling the app does not delete cloud-based Projects or threads.

Fix a Task That Is Stuck or Did Not Create a File

Check the task on the web

Open Perplexity in your browser and find the task. The task may have continued successfully even if the app stopped updating.

If the task is visible and progressing on the web, restart the app and reopen it there.

If the task is still stuck

  1. Stop the task.
  2. Confirm that the required device, folder, browser, installed app, or connector is available.
  3. Submit the request again.

Before repeating a request that moves, changes, or creates files, check whether any part of it already completed.

If a file is missing

  • Check the destination named in the request.
  • Open the completed task and review its result files, or check Artifacts.
  • Confirm that the task completed rather than stopping at an approval or error.

If the problem continues, use the main Perplexity Help Center support flow and include the task link, platform, app version, and a description of the last visible action.

Fix Crashes, Blank Windows, or High Resource Use

If the app will not open or shows a blank window

  • Close and reopen the app.
  • Confirm that the current version is installed.
  • Restart the computer if the app remains unavailable.

If the app becomes slow or uses excessive memory

  • Check whether a long-running task, browser session, or generated preview is still active.
  • Close completed tasks, previews, or browser windows that are no longer needed.
  • Restart the app.

If the problem continues, use the main Perplexity Help Center support flow.

Fix Local File and Folder Access

Use this article when the app cannot find, read, create, or change a local file, or when the folder option is missing.

Mac

  1. Open Settings → Permissions and confirm that local file access is enabled.
  2. Confirm that the required folder has been added.
  3. Review Full Disk Access, the broadest macOS permission for this, if the app still cannot access the folder.

Windows

  1. Confirm that the Sandbox has finished provisioning in Settings → Sandbox.
  2. Open Settings → Devices → Local file system access and confirm that access is enabled.
  3. Confirm that the required folder has been added.
  4. Confirm that the app is on its current update.
  5. Review the relevant Windows permission if the app still cannot access the folder.

If access worked before

  • Confirm that the file or folder has not been moved or renamed.
  • Remove and add the folder again.
  • Check whether the request needs a file outside the selected folder.
  • Restart the app.

Get Started

Install the app, sign in, and enable the desktop capabilities you want to use.

Install and update on Windows

Install from the Microsoft Store
  1. On a Windows device, open the Perplexity listing directly in the Microsoft Store: ms-windows-store://pdp/?productid=9P9XG917PWCJ.
  2. Select Install.
  3. Open Perplexity and sign in.
Install from the Perplexity website
  1. Open the Perplexity download page.
  2. Download and run the Windows installer.
  3. Open Perplexity and sign in.

The website installer uses the Microsoft Store installation and update system in the background. You do not need to open the Store separately during installation.

Check for updates

Updates get downloaded and happen automatically when the app restarts.

To check whether an update is available, open Settings → About, then select Open Microsoft Store.

Install and update on Mac

  1. Download the latest Perplexity app for Mac from the Perplexity download page.
  2. Open the downloaded DMG.
  3. Move Perplexity to Applications.
  4. Open Perplexity and sign in.
Complete Mac setup

For local files, browser tasks, installed apps, and remote work:

  1. Install Comet.
  2. Approve the macOS permissions requested by the app, including Accessibility, Screen & System Audio Recording, and Full Disk Access.
  3. Connect the folders and apps you want Perplexity to use.
  4. Pair your phone if you want to start work remotely or approve sensitive actions from it.

See the full Personal Computer setup guide.

Check for updates

Mac updates are not turned on automatically by default. Open Perplexity (in the top left OS menu bar) → Check for Updates to check whether an update is available. If an update is available, you can install it from there, and opt in to automatic updates using the checkbox when it is available.

Enable the Sandbox (Windows)

On Windows, local file access requires the Sandbox to be provisioned first. This happens in two stages:

  1. Provision the Sandbox. You are prompted to do this during onboarding, or you can provision it later in Settings → Sandbox.
  2. Enable local file system access. Once the Sandbox has finished provisioning, the local file access toggle in Settings → Devices → Local file system access becomes available. Select Enable to turn it on.

If the toggle is not available yet, check that the Sandbox has finished provisioning in Settings → Sandbox.

Enable local file access

The app may ask for local file access during onboarding or in a thread when your request needs files on the computer. You can also manage access in Settings:

  • Mac: Open Settings → Permissions.
  • Windows: After the Sandbox is provisioned, open Settings → Devices → Local file system access, then select Enable.

You can add different folders for different tasks and remove folders you no longer want the app to use.

For examples, see Work with local files and folders.

If installation does not complete

  • Windows: Confirm that you are using the current Microsoft Store link. If one installation entry point does not work, try the other: the Store listing or the Perplexity website.
  • Mac: Delete the existing copy of the app first. Then download a fresh copy of the current DMG and move the app to Applications before opening it.

If installation still does not complete, use the main Perplexity Help Center support flow.

Upgrade from the Old App

Older Windows and Mac apps do not upgrade to the new app automatically. Install the new app, sign in, then delete the old one.

Windows

  1. Install the new app from the Microsoft Store or the Perplexity website.
  2. Open it and sign in.
  3. Delete the old Perplexity app.

Mac

  1. Download and install the new app from the Perplexity website.
  2. Open it and sign in.
  3. Open Applications in Finder.
  4. Delete the older Perplexity app that was installed from the Mac App Store.

Deleting the old application does not delete your Perplexity account, Projects, or cloud-based threads.

Use the App on a Mac Mini

Perplexity’s desktop capabilities live inside the regular Perplexity macOS app — there is no separate Mac mini application. Install the regular Perplexity app on the Mac mini and sign in with your Perplexity account, the same way as on any other Mac. A supported Mac mini needs macOS 15 or later.

To have a device perform work, the regular Perplexity app must be installed, online, awake, and signed in on that specific Mac. Installing the app on one Mac does not make another Mac available. For example, if you plan to trigger work from your iPhone that should sometimes run on your Mac and sometimes on your Mac mini, both Macs need the app installed and signed in.

Follow the Personal Computer setup guide on each Mac: install Perplexity and Comet, approve the requested macOS permissions, connect the folders and apps the device should use, and pair your phone for remote starts and approvals.

Why use a Mac mini?

  • Keep a dedicated computer that can be left on at home for longer work, rather than tying up your primary laptop.
  • Use the local folders, Comet browser, and installed apps available on that Mac.
  • Start or continue work remotely from another Perplexity surface, approving sensitive steps from your phone while the Mac mini keeps working.

Keep the Mac mini available

Keep the Mac mini online, awake, and signed in when a task needs its local resources. A task may pause if the app closes, the Mac disconnects, or an action is waiting for approval.

Which computer performs the work?

When a task uses resources on the Mac mini, the actions happen on that Mac mini. Its local files, apps, browser state, permissions, and network connection determine what the task can use.

If several computers are available, choose the device that should perform the work. Another computer being awake does not automatically move the task away from the selected device.

Work with Browsers and Apps

Use the floating task bar

The floating task bar opens Perplexity while you work in another app. It is available on Windows and Mac.

Use it to start a request or bring relevant desktop context into Perplexity. Open the main app when you need the full task, file, or history view.

On Mac, the default shortcut is pressing the left and right Command keys together. Open Settings → Shortcuts to view or change the floating task bar and voice shortcuts. On Windows, use Settings → Shortcuts to view or change the configured shortcut.

Use voice

Voice lets you speak a request instead of typing it. It is available on Windows and Mac.

On Mac, hold 0 to start voice mode, then speak the task you want Perplexity to perform. On Windows, use 1+2+3.

Open Settings → Shortcuts to view or change the shortcut.

Use Incognito mode

Incognito mode keeps a search out of your saved history. Enable it by clicking a button in the top right corner of a new task page.

  • Incognito searches are not stored. Once a session is closed, it cannot be recovered.
  • Normal mode keeps your signed-in history, so your other searches and threads remain available.

Work with Comet

On Mac, install Comet so Perplexity can browse, research, and perform supported web actions. There is no separate Comet connector to enable in the app before first use. When a task needs the local browser, the app requests permission and then uses the installed Comet browser.

Work with installed apps

Installed-app control is available on Mac, not Windows. It can work through supported Mac apps such as Mail, Finder, Slack, Messages, and Notes.

Installed-app control is different from a connector:

  • An installed app is used through the application running on the Mac.
  • A connector gives Perplexity access to an account or service through an integration.

Features

Does Perplexity support conversation and chat?

Perplexity is primarily designed for finding and delivering accurate information, rather than engaging in extended conversation.

Email Assistant for Perplexity Max and Enterprise Max

Please note that Email Assistant will soon be deprecated.

The Email Assistant for Perplexity Max and Enterprise Max is a powerful new feature that transforms the way your email inboxes are managed, blending advanced AI organization with actionable productivity. It organizes your inbox, drafts replies automatically, and schedules meetings on your behalf, so you can focus on the work that matters.

Email Assistant works with Gmail and Outlook, delivering fast, focused, and secure communication with advanced data protection for both personal and enterprise needs.

The Email Assistant requires a Perplexity Max or Enterprise Max subscription.

You will need to upgrade to one of these plans to use the Email Assistant if you’re on any other subscription plan.
