How it works

The Email Assistant uses AI to analyze and understand the content, context, and urgency of incoming emails. Messages are automatically sorted into categories, urgent emails are highlighted for immediate attention, while spam and low-priority items are filtered out of view.

The system continuously adapts by learning from user behaviors, improving organization as more emails are processed.

The Email Assistant can draft contextual replies tailored to your writing style and specific conversations. You can review and edit these draft responses before sending—or you can enable automated replies for routine queries. Every edit you make helps refine future suggestions, ensuring the Assistant’s responses remain aligned with your individual preferences.

The Email Assistant can also manage your scheduling. When a someone requests a meeting with you via email, the Email Assistant checks your connected calendars, suggests available times, and sends invitations automatically. It manages time zones, and can reschedule if a conflict arises. This approach reduces scheduling friction and back-and-forth, and require minimal input on your part.

Is it secure?

Always-on drafting and labeling never logs your email content, and all email chains sent or received by the Assistant are deleted in 14 days.

Enterprise Max

In addition to enterprise-grade protection of your email data in transit and at rest, Enterprise users’ data, including your email and calendar data, is never used to train AI models—you retain absolute control over your data at all times.

Enterprise Max complies with GDPR, SOC 2, and other critical privacy regulations, with robust safeguards that meet the highest standards for confidentiality and compliance. Enterprise organizations can learn more in our Trust Center.

How to activate it

Currently, the Email Assistant only works with one email address. If you have your Google and Outlook, the Email Assistant will prioritize Google.

We’re working to enable the Email Assistant to work with multiple email addresses.

To activate the Email Assistant:

  1. Go to assistant
  2. Click Get Started
  3. Choose between connecting with Google or Outlook

If you haven’t already connected to your Google or Outlook account, you will be prompted to do so and grant some permissions to Perplexity to read and manage your account 4. Once you connected Perplexity to your email account, click Continue 5. You will then be able to enter your meeting availability, which the Email Assistant will use to schedule meetings on your behalf – you will be able to change this later 6. Next, you will be able to enable Smart Labels – a feature of the Email Assistant that organizes and labels your emails automatically 7. Then, you will be able to enable Smart Drafts, which allow the Email Assistant to draft responses to your emails using your voice and tone 8. Lastly, you’ll need to send an email to the assistant (assistant@perplexity.com) – you can do this from the onboarding screen

How to manage it

Once you have activated the Email Assistant, you will be able to manage it from your account Settings.

Here, you will be able to set your:

  • Full name: the name used in email signatures and calendar invites
  • Organization: your company or organization name
  • Meeting availability
  • Meeting length: you can customize the default meeting duration using different preset lengths and buffer duration, or choose your preferred meeting length
  • Timezone
  • Meeting locations: your preferred locations for meetings, and their addresses – this is useful to provide directions
  • Autonomy level: you can have the Email Assistant confirm with you before proposing times, before scheduling meetings, or schedule meetings for you without your input
  • Auto-draft: you can enable or disable the automatic generation of draft responses
  • Auto-label: you can enable or disable the automatic categorization and labelling of incoming emails
  • Label categories: you can choose which email labels the assistant can apply to your incoming emails

From this screen, you can also Personalize your assistant, by providing your specific preferences for email and schedule management.

You can also Disable the Email Assistant if you do not wish to use it anymore. You can reactivate later if you change your mind, by following the activation steps again.

File Uploads

You can upload files when starting a new session by clicking the “+ Attach” button in the search bar. We also support drag and drop for both files and folders. This enables Perplexity to access the file’s contents and maintain contextual awareness for follow-up questions within that session.

Short files can be analyzed in their entirety. If you upload a long file, Perplexity will extract the most important parts to provide the most relevant response to your query.

What types of files can be uploaded?

You can upload textual files, including plain text, code, or PDFs, as well as images, audio, and video files.

You can now upload both audio and video files to Perplexity as attachments and persistent files, using both connectors and local sources. Our system will automatically transcribe the spoken content into text, making it searchable via Perplexity.

What We Can Do

  • Transcribe speech to text from both audio and video files.
  • Identify and label speakers (ie: “who said what”) in the transcription.
  • Make the transcribed text searchable so you can easily find information via natural language.

What We Can’t Do

  • While we transcribe and make the audio content searchable, visual scenes from video files aren’t indexed or searchable at this time.

File Formats Supported: “audio/mpeg”, “audio/wav”, “audio/aiff”, “audio/ogg”, “audio/flac”, “audio/mp3”, “video/mp4”, “video/mpeg”, “video/mov”, “video/avi”, “video/x-flv”, “video/mpg”, “video/webm”, “video/wmv”, “video/3gpp”.

FAQs

What is the maximum file size for uploads?

File size is limited to 40 MB for all file types. ​

Who benefits from the file upload feature?

Researchers

Upload academic papers to extract key insights, summarize findings, and explore related topics.

Students

Get explanations of complex topics, summarize texts, and translate study materials.

Professionals

Analyze business reports, financial documents, and technical manuals to make informed decisions.

Developers

Upload code files to get explanations, debug code, and understand code snippets.

Writers

Proofread documents, get feedback on drafts, and brainstorm ideas.

Memory

What is Memory?

Memory is a feature that allows Perplexity to remember details between your conversations, making your interactions more personalized and efficient. As you engage with Perplexity, it learns your preferences and important information, letting Perplexity serve you with better, more personalized answers to your questions in the future.

Our goal is to give you the best answers possible. To do that, Perplexity may draw on:

  • Memories: The preferences, interests, and information you share with us.
  • Search history: Your past questions and answers that can help make a new response more relevant.

In Computer, Max subscribers also get Brain — a self-improving memory system that turns your sessions into a working model of your world. Memory makes Search personal; Brain gives Computer working context. They’re managed with separate controls.

When is memory and search history used?

Perplexity will call on memory and previous searches depending on the question and whether using these sources informs a better response. As always, Perplexity will cite any reference that determines the answer. You can toggle each independently in Settings → Memory.

How are memories created?

As you ask questions or share preferences, Perplexity stores key memories you can manage in Settings. Over time, this builds a memory bank that helps Perplexity give more personalized responses. The more you use Perplexity, the more useful it becomes. For example, if you often ask about Italian, Chinese, or Mediterranean recipes, Perplexity will remember the preference next time you ask for restaurant or recipe ideas.

How can I view my memories?

Go to Settings → Memory. You’ll see:

  • Use search history — toggle for referencing past searches.
  • Notes — toggle for saving simple details from your sessions for future answers.
  • Manage memories — opens your full memory bank, where you can search, filter, and delete individual entries.
  • Brain (Max subscribers) — toggle to build structured memories.

Can I remove certain memories or sessions from showing up in future answers?

Yes. Referenced memories appear in your answer’s sources — you can delete any from Settings → Memory → Manage memories to stop them from being used again. To remove a past session from influencing future answers, delete it from your search history.

Can I turn off referencing memory or search history?

You can toggle Memory and Search history off in your settings. When off, Perplexity stops referencing them in future answers. The permissions are separated, so you can control each independently. Turning Memory off does not clear existing memories. To clear memories, go into Memory → Manage memories.

Memory and previous searches are always off when using incognito mode, and questions asked in incognito are not retained or used in memory ever.

How can I delete my memories?

You can clear any specific memory at any time in settings by clicking on the trash icon. You can also clear all your memories at once by selecting the clear all button. If you delete a memory, that memory will be removed from the bank in settings and will not be used when responding to conversations moving forward. We may retain a log of cleared memories for up to 30 days for the purposes of safety, debugging, and ensuring memories aren’t re-created immediately after deletion.

How does Memory work in Projects?

Projects have two layers of memory that can work together:

  • Shared project memory (Brain) — Computer maintains this automatically from the project’s sessions and files, and everyone in the project sees the same Brain. It’s a wiki: contributors can edit it, viewers can read it, and the project owner controls the memory toggle. Brain is available in Computer sessions only. Search does not access Brain.
  • Personal memory in a project — a project’s shared Brain can be layered with the personal memory of whoever runs a session in it. The project owner controls this with the Use session creator’s memory toggle in the project’s Memory settings. When it’s on, each session in the project also draws on the personal memory of whoever started that session. When it’s off, sessions in the project only use the shared Brain. On Enterprise, admins can also turn memory access features off org-wide.

Does Memory retain sensitive information?

Memory is built with additional layers of privacy and safety in determining what types of information should be remembered and how it should be used.

We’ve taken steps to proactively reduce the ability for memory to retain sensitive information like your health details or financial information — but memory is generated based on a model that favors the frequency of questions asked and information given by the user. We will continue to improve our ability to detect and remove sensitive information, and you can always review and clear your memory bank.

Is Memory available to Enterprise Pro organizations?

Yes. Enterprise admins manage Memory at the organization level, memories belong to the organization, and additional privacy safeguards apply. Brain in Computer follows the same organization-level Memory setting by default. See Memory for Enterprise Organizations for details.

Are models trained on memories?

We may use content from memory to improve our models for everyone. Your specific data isn’t shared with anyone, and if you’d like, you can turn off our ability to use your data by toggling off AI Data Retention in Settings → Preferences → Artificial Intelligence.

Scheduled Tasks in Computer

What are Scheduled Tasks in Computer?

Scheduled Tasks let Perplexity Computer run recurring work for you in the background — monitoring, reports, alerts, inbox checks, competitive tracking, daily briefings, and more — on a schedule you set. Computer runs each task autonomously in the cloud, so your laptop doesn’t need to be open. When something noteworthy happens, Computer sends you a notification and saves the full result to the task’s session.

Scheduled Tasks live inside Computer at Computer → Tasks (0). They replace the older Perplexity Tasks experience and are built on Computer’s agent system, which means a scheduled task can use the same tools, connectors, and subagents that you’d use in a one-off Computer conversation.

Note: Scheduled Tasks in Computer are different from one-time reminders. If you just want Computer to do something once at a specific time (“remind me at 4pm”, “send this email tomorrow at 9am”), Computer handles that as a one-time wait, not a recurring task.

Creating a Scheduled Task

You don’t fill out a fixed form. You describe what you want in plain language inside Computer, and Computer proposes a schedule for your confirmation.

  1. Open Computer from the left sidebar (or go to 0).
  2. In the input box, describe the recurring task and the cadence. For example:
  • “Every weekday at 8am, summarize new posts from My First Million, All In, and 20VC and email me the highlights.”
  • “Check my Gmail every hour for replies from investors and notify me when one arrives.”
  • “Every Monday at 9am, pull our sales pipeline from Salesforce and post a summary to #revenue in Slack.”
  1. Computer will confirm the schedule, what the task will do, and remind you that each run uses credits. Approve to create the task.
  2. Your task now appears under Scheduled on the Computer dashboard, with the next run time shown.

You can also create a scheduled task from inside any existing Computer conversation — just ask Computer to “run this every morning” (or whatever cadence) and it will schedule the recurring version of that work.

Supported schedules

Scheduled Tasks support any recurring cadence as long as it runs no more often than once per hour. Common cadences:

  • Hourly
  • Daily (at a specific time)
  • Every weekday
  • Weekly (specific day + time)
  • Monthly
  • Custom recurring schedules

Computer automatically converts the time you give it (in your local timezone) into the underlying schedule, and shows you the next run time in your timezone on the dashboard.

Not supported as a Scheduled Task:

  • Sub-hourly cadences (more often than once per hour).
  • One-time actions — use a one-time reminder instead (“remind me once tomorrow at 9am”).

Background vs. attended tasks

Most scheduled tasks run in the background, meaning Computer spins up a fresh, isolated agent for each run with no prior conversation context. This is ideal for:

  • Monitoring (prices, news, mentions, inboxes)
  • Recurring reports and briefings
  • Alerts and notifications
  • Data collection and tracking

Some tasks need to run attended (with conversation context or richer tooling). Computer will pick this automatically when the task needs to:

  • Generate a document (PDF, Word, PowerPoint, Excel)
  • Drive a browser (book something, fill a form, log into a site)
  • Publish or update a hosted website
  • Build on context from the conversation that created it

If a background task gets stuck — for example, instructions are unclear or it needs your input — it will surface a Needs attention state on the dashboard and notify you instead of guessing.

Using connectors in Scheduled Tasks

Scheduled Tasks can use any connector you’ve linked to Computer (Gmail, Google Calendar, Slack, Notion, Linear, GitHub, Outlook, Microsoft Teams, Jira, Confluence, Asana, Salesforce, Stripe, Google Drive, OneDrive, SharePoint, Dropbox, Box, and more). Manage these from Computer → Connectors.

If a connector becomes disconnected or its authorization expires, Computer will surface a reconnect prompt the next time the task runs. After repeated failures on the same connector issue, Computer may stop the task so it doesn’t keep using credits while blocked — you’ll be notified and can recreate it once you’ve reconnected.

Notifications

When a scheduled task produces something new or noteworthy, Computer notifies you. Available channels:

  • In-app (default)
  • Mobile push (via the Perplexity iOS/Android app)
  • Web push
  • Email

Manage notification preferences under Settings → Notifications. Each notification deep-links straight to the task’s session so you can review the full result and any artifacts.

If a run finds nothing new (for example, an hourly inbox check with no new replies), Computer stays quiet — you only get notified when there’s something worth your attention.

Managing your Scheduled Tasks

Open Computer → Tasks to see all your scheduled tasks, grouped by status (Scheduled, Needs attention, Completed). Click any task to open its detail page.

From the task detail page, or from inside the conversation that owns the task, you can:

  • See the next run time and recent run history
  • Review past results and notifications
  • Update the schedule or the instructions (“change this to every weekday at 7am instead”, “also include TechCrunch in the sources”)
  • Stop the task

Stopping a task removes it permanently. There is no separate paused state — if you want to bring a task back later, recreate it. You can also stop a task from the conversation’s right-hand context pane using the Stop task button.

Tasks from other conversations

Each Computer conversation can own up to 15 scheduled tasks. Tasks created in one conversation are managed from that conversation. To find and manage a task created elsewhere, go to Computer → Tasks for a unified view, or open the task’s URL directly at 0.

Credits and billing

Each time a scheduled task runs, it uses Computer credits — just like any other Computer work. A few things to keep in mind:

  • Computer will tell you the schedule and remind you that each run uses credits before creating the task.
  • If your account runs out of credits, or doesn’t have Computer access at the moment of a scheduled run, that run will be skipped, automatically retried up to a few times, and then resume on the normal schedule once credits are available.
  • Max and Enterprise Max plans include monthly complimentary credits for Computer tasks. See your plan details in Settings → Subscription.

To control spend, keep schedules at the lowest cadence that meets your need (hourly checks use more credits than daily ones), and stop tasks you no longer need.

Troubleshooting

My task didn’t run when I expected.

Background tasks may take a few minutes to start after their scheduled time. If a run was skipped, check the task’s detail page — common reasons are insufficient credits, an expired connector authorization, or the task hitting a “Needs attention” state.

I’m getting too many notifications.

Open the task and either lower the cadence (“change this to once a day instead of hourly”) or tell Computer to only notify on specific conditions (“only alert me if the price drops more than 5%”).

I can’t find a task I created.

Go to Computer → Tasks for a list across all your conversations. If the task was created in another conversation, you can also open it directly at 0.

I want to pause a task temporarily.

There’s no pause state today — stop the task and recreate it when you’re ready. We’re tracking this as a potential improvement.

Availability

Scheduled Tasks in Computer are available wherever Computer is available. Specific plan-tier limits on the number of active scheduled tasks are managed at the conversation level (up to 15 per conversation). For plan-level Computer access and credit allowances, see your subscription details.

Uploading Images on Perplexity

Enhance your search experience by uploading an image to support your query. The image upload feature allows Perplexity to identify images, interpret graphics, or generate the perfect social media captions.

How to upload on desktop

Click the Attach button when starting a new thread or simply drag and drop the image into the search bar.

How to upload on mobile

Below the search bar, click the + icon. Select an image from your gallery or take a new photo.

Technical Specifications

Accepted formats

JPEG, HEF, PNG, PDF.

Maximum image size

Up to 40MB. Perplexity will reformat the image to meet necessary standards.

Usage guidelines

Image processing

We utilize advanced multimodal models to analyze images. Rest assured, your images are not stored on our servers, ensuring your privacy.

Types of images

Avoid uploading medical images, upside-down or rotated images, precise spatial locations, panoramic images, explicit content, and pictures of individuals.

Troubleshooting

If Perplexity can’t read your image, make sure it’s in a supported format and doesn’t contain explicit content or identifiable individuals. Also, check your network connection. For ongoing issues, email us at support@perplexity.ai with:

  • Description of the issue and image
  • URL to the thread
  • Optionally, the image

Additional information

Usage examples

Use image upload to identify objects, interpret charts, or generate captions for social media.

View examples.

Access and privacy

By default, the search will only use the image as a source. To enable web browsing, turn on Pro Search.

What are Projects?

What is a Project?

A Project is a persistent, shareable workspace in Perplexity that keeps everything for an ongoing effort in one hub — your Search conversations, your Computer tasks, files, custom instructions, connected tools, and the context Perplexity builds up as you work. Instead of one-off tasks, a Project lets you and any collaborators return to the same body of work over time.

Creating a Project

  • Click the Projects section in the left-side panel to open up your projects menu, then click + New project. You can also click on the + hover option in the Projects header
  • Give your Project a title (up to 50 characters), an optional description (up to 1,000 characters), and an optional icon/color to make it easy to recognize.
  • Add instructions (up to 8,000 characters) that tell Computer how to work inside this Project — for example, a preferred response format or a status file to keep updated.
  • Projects are private (Restricted) by default. You choose who to share with in the next step.

You can also start a Project from an existing session, and Perplexity will suggest a title, description, and instructions based on it.

In a Computer task, use Convert session to project to spin up a Project and carry your files and artifacts into it or just ask Computer to create a project from an ongoing session or from existing sessions.

Collaboration and sharing

Click on the portrait list of project members on the top right of a project, or the Share button to manage access and sharing permissions.

Roles

  • Owner (full control)
  • Can edit (edit context, files, connectors, skills, people, defaults, channels)
  • Can view (read the Project and shared sessions/files).

Access scopes

  • Restricted (invited people only — the default)
  • Organization can view
  • Organization can edit (Enterprise)
  • Anyone with the link can view (if your org allows public sharing).

Contributor limits: up to 5 contributors on non-Enterprise Projects; up to 9,999 on Enterprise-owned Projects.

Enterprise controls: admins can restrict public sharing, external invites, and org-wide publishing.

Project files

Each Project has access to Files. You can upload persistent files individually, as folders, and as imports from connected file sources to serve context in a project. Computer can also create, update, and manage files on your behalf.

Files in a project are shared with project members with the appropriate permissions. Viewers and editors can view files but only editors can edit project files.

Brain

Brain helps build ongoing memory for all work done in a project. You can view the current memory generated in the Brain tab.

Brain runs are billed to the creator of the project and you can control whether it runs automatically or manually in the memory section in the Settings tab.

Project settings

Additional context can be added and managed in the Settings tab and can be configured by the project owner.

  • Context

  • Add and manage instructions that are used for every query run in the project

  • Add and manage web links and domains to be prioritized

  • Manage the default mode (Search vs. Computer) and set a default Computer orchestrator model

  • Memory

  • Enable and manage project level Brain settings. Brain builds a live and updating knowledge based on the project’s activity to give Computer better context

  • Enable and manage project summary settings. Project summary generates a running status update of the work done in a project. It enabled, it will also leverage Brain for more context

  • Control whether queries in the project are allowed to draw from individual memory

  • Slack and Teams

  • Bind Slack or Teams channels to this project, ensuring that context from these channels in included in project context and that any Computer queries submitted in those channels show up in the session history of this project

  • Connectors

  • Choose which connectors the project uses and add connector-specific instructions

  • Add shared project-level credentials

  • Skills

  • Upload and manage reusable skills scoped to the project. Note that users will still have access to personal and organization skills within a project as well

Organizing your Projects

Pin important Projects for quick access. Pinned projects show up in the project overview page as well as in the Projects section in the left-side bar.

What is a Session?

What is a Session?

A session is like a conversation history that remembers everything you’ve discussed with Perplexity’s AI. Unlike traditional search queries that operate in isolation, sessions enable seamless, contextual conversations that build upon previous exchanges within the session. A session will include your initial question, any follow-up queries, all of Perplexity’s responses and all sources used to provide your answer.

Core Functions of Sessions

Seamless Conversation

Sessions work like a conversation project that keeps track of all the messages you exchange with Perplexity, remembering what you’ve talked about and keeping your discussion flowing smoothly from one question to the next. When you ask a follow-up question or when the AI gives you a response, it understands what you’ve been discussing together throughout your entire conversation,. This way you receive answers that make more sense and are more helpful for what you’re trying to learn or accomplish.

Persistent Memory

Because the AI has “memory” it can automatically handle context management even as conversations grow longer. This eliminates the need for you to manually repeat background information or context in subsequent queries.

Source Transparency

By weaving reliable sources directly into every session, Perplexity turns answers into traceable mini-research papers: transparent, verifiable, and easy to build upon. This ensures higher answer quality.

What can I use Sessions for?

Deep Topic Exploration

You can ask follow-up questions to dig deeper or explore new angles within a single thread. This allows for comprehensive exploration of all sorts of topics without losing the contextestablished in earlier exchanges.

Iterative Problem Solving

Sessions support iterative workflows where you can:

  • Build upon previous responses to refine your understanding of the topic
  • Ask clarifying questions that reference earlier parts of the conversation
  • Develop ideas progressively through multiple exchanges
Context-Aware Research

Since threads ensure that the conversation history is maintained, you can conduct research where each query builds upon previous findings, creating a more coherent and comprehensive research experience.

Your Sessions History

Every session is stored in yourHistory (../assets/img/a5c9b93551_library)which is only accessible to you. In your History you can view previous sessions and continue earlier conversations.

Sessions are stored in the History indefinitely but you can delete individual or all sessions at any time. Furthermore, you can filter your sessions by Search Mode (Pro, Research, or Create files and apps query) and from newest to oldest and vice versa.

If you use the PerplexityTasksfeature your Tasks sessions will also show in your History.

Creating, Managing and Sharing Sessions

Creating Sessions

Creating a session is simple - to get started, go to the Search query boxon your homepage and start typing your question or ask i.e. your prompt.

If you’re a paid subscriber you will also have the option to select from different Search Modes: Pro, Research, and Create files and apps (1) and select your AI model of choice (2). You can also specify your Sources, like Web or Academic (3) and attach files (4). You can also dictate your prompt - just click on microphone icon (5).

Managing your Sessions

To manage your sessions go to to your History page: ../assets/img/a5c9b93551_library.

In your History:

  • You will be able to see all of your sessions and use the Search bar to find specific sessions
  • Click Select to select multiple sessions for deletion
  • Use Typeto sort sessions by Search Mode (sessions created in Research or Create files and apps)
  • Hide or show sessions created via Comet browser
  • Use Sorting to sort from Newest to Oldest and vice versa
  • To delete all sessions in one go click on the dots menu on the right side of the screen:
  • Alternatively, you can click the three dots menu on a specific session to either delete the session or Add it to a project:
Sharing your Sessions and more actions

To share a session click on the Share button on the top right side of the screen:

You can select from a few sharing options:

  • Private:The session stays private (only you as the Author can view it’s content) even if you have pasted the link somewhere publicly
  • Anyone with the link:anyone who has the link can open and view the content of the session and also share it on to others. If you no longer want the session to be public, you can change it back to Private (see above)
  • Anyone in my org with the link (for Enterprise Pro subscribers only)

When a session is public,anyone with access to the session can also add follow-up questions to but this will create a new session for the user (it won’t add the follow-up to the existing session).

More actions

  • Export button (1) allows you to export the answer to the session as PDF, Markdown, or DOCX
  • Rewrite button(2) generates a new answer using a different model that you can select
  • Source button(3) tells you info about which Model, Search mode and Source mode was appliedto the answer
  • Feedback (4) share with a thumbs up/down if you’re satisfied or dissatisfied with the answerprovided
  • Copy button(5) copies the answer to your clipboard
  • Three dots menu(6): click this to view Sources, Report feedback or delete that portion of the session
  • To edit the original query, hover over the prompt and the edit icon will appead

FAQs

Can I recover a deleted Session?

Once a session is deleted, it cannot be retrieved. To prevent accidental deletions, we’ve added confirmation prompts.

What happens to Sessions when I’m not signed in?

When you create sessions while not signed into your Perplexity account, the author of the session is considered “anonymous,” and therefore, the session is not saved long-term. These anonymous sessions will be visible for 14 days, after which they will disappear permanently without the possibility of recovery.

To ensure that a session is accessible long-term, please sign into your account. All sessions created while signed in will be automatically saved to your session History.

Are my Sessions private?

Yes, your sessions are private and only for your reference by default. They only become public if you choose to share them.

What is Learn Mode?

Learn Mode makes your learning journey interactive, helping you study better and master material rather than just get the answer.

This search mode is available for all users, including free and logged-out users.

How do I access Learn Mode?

At the moment, learn mode is only available via the toggle to verified students.

For verified students:

If you’re a student who has verified your student status, you’ll see a Learn icon in the search mode toggles. (This is only available on an account that has verified student status. Scroll to the end of this article to learn how to verify as a student.)

For Students on the web app

To activate Learn Mode for your Search, click on Learn icon on the toggle panel in your Search bar.

For Students in Comet

In a new tab on Comet, click “Edit widgets” and click “Shortcut”. Edit or create a shortcut to use “Learn Mode” under the “Advanced” toggle.

What is Learn Mode?

Learn Mode is Perplexity’s Search, but optimized for active learning. Instead of giving just the answer to your question, Learn Mode explains the answer in detail, breaking it down step-by-step. With Learn Mode you can:

  1. Learn through conversation rather than getting an instant answer

  2. Break down your learning step-by-step

  3. Generate custom study tailored to your courses and study notes inline in session, like interactive free-response exercises, flashcards, and multiple choice quizzes.

  4. Interactive Flashcards

  5. Interactive Multiple Choice Quizzes

Key features:

Guided Learning: Instead of just giving you the answers, Learn Mode will ask thoughtful questions and provide gentle hints to help you discover solutions on your own, turning the learning process into an experience both engaging and memorable.

Step-by-Step Explanations: Learn Mode breaks down complex topics into clear, manageable chunks that show how ideas connect to each other, so you won’t feel overwhelmed by too much information at once.

Learning That Fits You: Learn Mode adjusts its teaching style to match your skill level by asking a few quick questions to understand what you already know and remember from your previous conversations.

Progress Tracking: Learn Mode will include mini-quizzes and thought-provoking questions with helpful feedback to help you see how you’re improving and make sure you can use what you’ve learned in real situations.

Easy Control: You can turn Learn Mode on or off whenever you want during your chat, so you can switch between casual conversation and focused learning as your needs change.

Example Use Cases

  1. (Example thread) Understanding a math problem from a problem set. In Learn Mode, take a screenshot of the problem and attach it in your query. Learn mode will help you solve the problem step-by-step.
  2. (Example thread) Learning about a new topic. Use Learn Mode to dive deeper and ask questions about what you don’t understand, tailored to your baseline familiarity level.
  3. (Example thread) Making flashcards about an upcoming psychology quiz. In Learn Mode, upload any course materials, readings, study guides, or lecture notes and ask Perplexity to create flashcards based on the attached materials.
  4. (Example thread) Creating practice quizzes tailored to what you’re learning to study for an upcoming exam. In Learn Mode, upload any course materials, readings, study guides, or lecture notes and ask Perplexity to create flashcards based on the attached materials.

Verifying as a Student

Learn Mode will be available for all verified student accounts. We verify student accounts through SheerID, our third-party verification partner. To access student verification, click “upgrade” in the bottom left corner and verify student status via the “Student” toggle on the paywall. You may use any email for Perplexity, not just a student email.

Click “upgrade” in the bottom left corner

Verify student status via the “Student” toggle on the paywall. (Available under the Education plan)

What is Perplexity Health?

Perplexity Health is a personalized health experience available to Pro and Max subscribers in the US. It lets you connect data from Medical Records, wearables, wellness apps, and Apple Health so Perplexity can provide context-aware answers to your health questions. It also powers a Health hub dashboard with fitness tracking, biomarker trends, AI-generated summaries, and health memories that give you a holistic view of your health information. Perplexity Health is designed for everyday informational use and is not intended to diagnose conditions or replace professional medical care.

Who can use Perplexity Health?

At launch, Perplexity Health will be included with Perplexity Pro and Max subscriptions in the US and is available to eligible subscribers on iOS and on the web at health. This feature is rolling out as part of a phased launch, so it may not be available to all eligible subscribers right away. Apple HealthKit-based features require you to be 18 years or older and are only available on iOS.

How do I access Perplexity Health?

The Perplexity Health hub is available on the web via the sidebar or at health. On iOS, you can find it by typing “Health” in the search bar. You can also ask health-related questions directly in the main chat — relevant queries will automatically trigger Perplexity Health.

What health data can I connect to Perplexity Health?

You can securely connect your Electronic Health Records through our partner b.well, your wellness apps via Terra API, and upload files to a dedicated and secure Health files repository. On your iOS device, you can also connect Apple Health.

What does the Health hub include?

The Health hub at health brings together your connected health data so you can track trends and get proactive insights. It features fitness tracking, biomarker tracking, activity logs, and AI-powered summaries. From the Health hub, you can connect Medical Records (via b.well), wellness apps (via Terra API), and upload health files directly.

Using Perplexity Health

What can I ask Perplexity Health?

You can ask general health questions as well as questions that use your own data, such as:

  • How did my sleep this week compare to last week?
  • Analyze my last 5 workouts and tell me how to improve.
  • What are my step trends over the past month?
  • Create a nutrition plan based on my lab data and activity levels.

When your data is available and your question is health-related, Perplexity Health references your relevant health data instead of providing only generic explanations.

How does Perplexity Health personalize responses?

When you choose to opt in and connect sources such as wellness apps or Medical Records, Perplexity uses relevant data from those providers, along with general population health insights to inform its answers. It also creates health memories that can be referenced for related queries, helping provide more personalized, context-aware responses. Personalized in this context means responses are adjusted based on your connected data and broader medical knowledge, not that Perplexity is delivering the kind of individualized assessment, diagnosis, or treatment advice a doctor can provide.

What health topics will Perplexity Health NOT answer?

Perplexity Health is designed for informational purposes only. It will not:

  • Provide medical diagnoses.
  • Recommend specific treatments.
  • Provide personalized nutrition therapy.
  • Give emergency medical advice.

For these topics, Perplexity Health always recommends consulting with a qualified healthcare provider.

Connecting Your Health Data

What are Medical Records?

Medical Records let Perplexity securely connect to your healthcare provider so your clinical history, labs, and visit information can be used to personalize health-related answers and your health dashboard.

Which countries are Medical Records available in?

Medical Records are available only in the United States at launch.

How do I connect Medical Records and what data is shared?

You can connect your Medical Records from the Health landing page, during onboarding, or from the Connectors section in Settings by selecting Medical Records. Once connected, Perplexity may access data such as lab results, visit summaries, medications, and medical conditions, depending on what your provider makes available.

Who powers the Medical Records connection?

The Medical Records connection is powered by b.well, a HIPAA-compliant platform that securely manages your health data connection between Perplexity and your healthcare providers. You can read their privacy policy here.

My health records data isn’t syncing — what should I do?

First, confirm that your Medical Records provider shows as active on the Health landing page and in Settings.

If it appears connected but no new data is arriving, try disconnecting and reconnecting the connector from Settings, then wait a few minutes for data to refresh; if the issue persists, submit a support ticket.

What is Connector in Perplexity Health?

Connector lets you link health devices and apps—such as Fitbit, Google, and Clue—so Perplexity can use your activity, sleep, recovery, and cycle data to personalize insights.

Who powers the health devices and apps connection?

The health devices and apps connection is powered by Terra API, a unified health and fitness data platform that securely connects Perplexity to your wearables and wellness apps. You can read their privacy policy here.

How do I connect wearables and what data is shared?

You can connect wearables during onboarding, or from the Health landing page by selecting Connectors > Health Devices and Apps. ​

Depending on the app and permissions you grant, Perplexity may receive data such as steps, workouts, heart rate, sleep, recovery, and other wellness metrics, which are used to ground health answers and recommendations.

My fitness and wearables data isn’t syncing — what should I do?

Start by checking that your wearable app (for example Fitbit) is up to date and has recently synced data in its own app. ​

Then confirm that the data source shows as active on the Health landing page or Connectors page, and, if needed, disconnect and reconnect the wearable; if data still does not appear after a short delay, submit a support ticket.

What is Apple Health in Perplexity?

Apple Health can be connected to help Perplexity Health understand the activity, sleep, movement, and other wellness signals you choose to share. Currently, Apple Health data is used to personalize your health answers but is not reflected on the Health hub dashboard or data visualizations.

Do I need an iPhone to use Apple Health?

Yes, you will need an iPhone to connect Apple Health.

How do I connect to Apple Health and what data is shared?

You’ll be prompted to connect Apple Health during the iOS onboarding flow when you ask relevant health questions. You can also enable it from the Connectors page. ​

Only data you select on the Apple Health permissions screen will be shared with Perplexity.

My Apple Health data isn’t syncing — what should I do?

First, confirm that Perplexity still has permission to read Apple Health data and that you are using the latest version of the Perplexity app. If your data still does not sync after checking permissions and waiting for a refresh period, try the following steps:

  1. Check your iOS Health permissions by opening the Health app > tap your profile picture (top right) > Apps > Perplexity.
  2. Make sure the Perplexity app has Background App Refresh enabled (Settings > General > Background App Refresh).
  3. Try disconnecting and reconnecting Apple Health from Perplexity Settings.
  4. If the issue persists, submit a support ticket.

Privacy & Data Security

Is my health data used to train AI models?

No. Your health chats, connector data, files, and memories are not used to train AI models and will never be sold to third parties. We also may use your questions and answers to help improve Perplexity, as explained in our Privacy Notice.

How is my health data stored and protected?

Your raw health data is queried on demand through our data providers and is not stored permanently on our servers. Any health files you upload are stored in a separate, encrypted health file repository.

To provide personalized insights, the system may generate health memories based on your queries. Your health information is accessed only when directly relevant to your request, and we use standard encryption along with HIPAA-aligned safeguards to protect your data.

Is my health information shared with other Perplexity features?

Health memories are referenced only when they are relevant to your question. Privacy safeguards are in place to limit how and when your health information is used.

Does Perplexity Health comply with HIPAA?

Perplexity Health is designed for personal wellness. HIPAA does not apply to consumer health products like Perplexity Health. However, your data is protected with end-to-end encryption and strict security practices that Perplexity follows.

​

Perplexity is SOC 2 Type II certified by independent auditors, ensuring your information remains protected at all times. We are also GDPR compliant. Your data is handled under strict European privacy standards with transparent data collection practices and full user control over personal information.

We do not train on your health data or ever sell your data to third parties.

Can I share my Health conversations?

You can share Health conversations on the web by selecting Share in the top right, adjusting the privacy settings, and copying the link. On iOS, tap ⋯ in the top-right corner of the conversation and select Share.

Managing Your Data & Health Memories

How do I view or delete my health memories?

You can view your health-related memories by going to Settings > Personalization > Manage your saved memories and filtering for “Health.” You can also delete a health memory by hovering over it and clicking the Delete icon.

I connected to a data source but did not see my data.

Your data should sync within a few minutes after connecting. If your data is missing, try disconnecting and reconnecting your data source from Settings. If the issue persists, submit a support ticket.

My health-related questions aren’t using my data.

If answers look generic rather than personalized, first confirm your health data sources are connected and the relevant permissions are granted. If those look correct, try disconnecting and reconnecting your data source from Settings. If the issue persists, submit a support ticket.

How do I disconnect my Health Connectors?

You can disconnect your wearable connector, health records connector, and Apple Health (iOS only) at any time by going to Settings > Connectors. You can also disable them directly from your Health hub.

How do I delete my health data?

You can delete your health memories in Personalization and remove connector access under “Connectors” on your Settings page. You can also delete any Health session at any time in your History tab.

What happens when I disconnect a data source?

When you disconnect a health connector, Perplexity stops receiving new data from that source. Perplexity does not store data from your connected sources and only retains information that appears in your sessions, which you can delete at any time.

What is Pro Search?

What is Pro Search?

Pro Search is Perplexity AI’s advanced search feature, designed to deliver nuanced, thorough answers to complex questions within seconds. The answer is devised by synthesizing information from a diverse and high-quality set of sources. This goes far beyond standard keyword-based searches, functioning more like a knowledgeable search assistant than a traditional search engine.

The Step-by-step process:

  • Model Selection: Before starting a search (session) Pro users can choose from several state-of-the-art AI models—including Perplexity’s own Sonar, OpenAI’s GPT-5, Claude Sonnet 4.6, and Gemini 3.1 Pro—each optimized for different types of queries.
  • Web Crawling: The system then conducts multiple searches across the web, drawing from articles, academic papers, forums, videos, and more, depending on your selected focus (e.g., Web, Academic, Finance, Files).
  • Synthesis and Summarization: The AI reads, analyzes, and compiles insights from dozens of sources and summarizes the information into a coherent, well-organized answer. You see not just the summary, but also how the AI broke down your question and approached the research.
  • Citation and Transparency: Every answer includes direct links to the original sources, allowing you to verify facts or explore further.
  • Interactive Refinement: You can continue the conversation with follow-up questions; Pro Search maintains context from previous interactions, enabling a natural, flowing dialogue.

Benefits of Pro Search

  • Solves Complex Problems: Pro Search excels at breaking down ambiguous or multifaceted questions, providing detailed, actionable answers
  • Saves Time: By compiling and summarizing the best insights from many sources, it eliminates the need to sift through pages of search results
  • Trustworthy: With full source citations, you can always check the origin of any information provided
  • Adaptable: Works for technical, academic, creative, and everyday questions—adjusting its approach based on your needs and the nature of your query
  • It can interpret code: Pro Search includes a code interpreter that allows users to execute code and obtain fast results. This feature is particularly useful for data analysis, debugging, and content generation, making it the perfect tool for developers and data scientists.

Advanced Capabilities

  • Reasoning models: For advanced analysis on any topic you can switch to the latest reasoning models like Anthropic’s Claude Sonnet 4.6 Thinking.
  • Code Interpretation: Pro Search includes a code interpreter for executing and analyzing code snippets, making it useful for developers and data scientists.
  • Search Modes: You can narrow your search to specific content types (e.g., Academic, Finance, your own files), ensuring answers are drawn from the most relevant sources for your needs.
  • Personalization: The feature adapts to your preferences and refines answers based on your feedback during the conversation.

What are some use cases for Pro Search?

Academic research

Pro Search can function as a research assistant for students and academics. It digs through academic papers and databases, runs simulations, and performs data analysis to tailor its findings, providing summarized papers and curated sources. It’s the smartest way to research.

Professional research

Pro search can pinpoint cases for attorneys, summarize trend analysis for marketers, and debug code for developers—and that’s just the start.

Code interpretation

Pro Search is a powerful tool for developers and programmers. It can debug code, run simulations, and provide detailed explanations for complex programming concepts. Whether you’re tackling a tricky bug or optimizing your code, Pro Search has you covered.

Validating information

Remember, while Pro search aims to elevate your quest for knowledge, it’s crucial to validate information by referencing the sources linked in your the answer.

Pro Search vs. Standard Search

  • Feature
  • Standard Search
  • Pro Search
  • Speed
  • Fast, basic answers
  • Slower, in-depth answers
  • Depth
  • Surface-level, 1–2 sources
  • Comprehensive, dozens of sources, Source databases
  • Model Options
  • Limited
  • Multiple advanced models to choose from
  • Source Transparency
  • Basic citations
  • Extensive, direct source links
  • Special Features
  • None
  • Code interpreter, Create files and apps access

Standard search is best for quick facts; Pro Search is ideal for research, analysis, and complex queries where depth, accuracy, and source transparency matter most.

What is Reasoning mode?

This mode is excellent when you need more analytical depth for your searches.

To choose any of these models simply select your preferred model and toggle reasoning:

  • Claude 4.6 Sonnet:Anthropic’s reasoning model

  • Claude 4.6 Opus: Anthropic’s most advanced reasoning model. Only available to Max plan subscribers

These models are specifically designed to handle complex analytical questions that may require multiple search steps or detailed breakdown of complicated topics.

What is Research mode?

It performs dozens of searches, reads hundreds of sources, and reasons through the material to autonomously deliver a comprehensive report and completes most research tasks in under 3 minutes which means it can take around 4 to 5 minutes to generate a response.

How It Works

  • Research with reasoning: Uses search and coding capabilities to iteratively search, read documents, and reason about next steps, “refining its research plan as it learns more about the subject areas”
  • Report writing: After evaluating source materials, “synthesizes all the research into a clear and comprehensive report”
  • Export & Share: Allows you to export the final report to PDF or document, or convert it to a Perplexity Page to share with others

When to Use Research

  • Excels at expert-level analysis across complex subject matters including:

  • Finance

  • Marketing

  • Technology

  • Current affairs

  • Health

  • Biography

  • Travel planning1

Can I pick a model when using Research?

Research uses a specific combination of models to generate in-depth reports for complex topics. This mode automatically selects the optimal models for your research needs, so you cannot manually choose a specific model when using Research

How does Perplexity work?

When you ask Perplexity a question, it uses advanced AI to search the internet in real-time, gathering insights from top-tier sources. It then distills this information into a clear, concise summary, delivering exactly what you need in an easy-to-understand, conversational tone.

How It Works

Perplexity goes beyond simple keyword searches. It uses cutting-edge language models like GPT-5 and Claude 4.6 Sonnet to understand the context and nuances of your query. Here’s a breakdown of the process:

Understanding Your Question

Perplexity leverages sophisticated AI to interpret your question, ensuring it knows exactly what you’re asking.

Searching the Web

It searches the internet, gathering information from authoritative sources like articles, websites, and journals.

Summarizing Information

Perplexity compiles the most relevant insights into a coherent, easy-to-understand answer.

Citing Sources

Each answer includes numbered citations linking to the original sources, allowing you to easily verify the information or explore further.

Prompting tips and examples

A prompt is the starting point for each of your conversations—or sessions—with Perplexity.Writing a clear and effective prompt is key to getting accurate and relevant information.

To get the best answers, make sure your prompt includes these elements:

  • Instruction: Clearly say what you want Perplexity to do.

  • Context: Give some background information to help Perplexity understand the task.

  • Input: Include any specific data or text Perplexity needs to answer your query.

  • Keywords: Use key terms that will help Perplexity focus on the most relevant details.

  • Output format: Explain how you want the response to be formatted.

  • Types of prompts:

  • Informational prompts: When you need specific information or facts. For Example: “What is a solar eclipse?”

  • Instructional prompts: When you need step-by-step instructions for completing a task. For example: “How can I remove a red wine stain from a white cotton shirt?”

  • Interactive prompts: When you want a conversational approach. For example: “Pretend you’re a hiring manager at a software company, and I’m interviewing for a job in quality assurance.”

For the best results with Perplexity, start with a clear goal, use straightforward language, and provide enough background so the task is understood. Test and tweak your prompts as needed, and avoid common pitfalls like being vague, asking for too much at once, or leaving out important context.

A few more prompt examples

Legal compliance

I run an online store and need you to provide a list of regulations affecting e-commerce businesses in Europe. Focus on data protection, consumer rights, and tax obligations.

Essay help for students

I’m a student aiming to excel in my psychology paper and need you to provide an essay outline on the effects of social media on mental health. Focus on areas such as anxiety, depression, and self-esteem, and include key studies and statistics.

Healthy meal planning

Please create a 7-day gluten-free meal plan under 2,000 calories per day, with balanced nutrition, high protein, varied fruits and vegetables, specific recipes for all meals and snacks, and a weekly shopping list.

Tips for Getting Better Answers from Perplexity

The golden rule: State your intent clearly and provide detail. Tell Perplexity exactly what you want to do — create an image, generate a video, search for information, build a document. The more specific information you include in your prompt, the better the results you’ll get. ​ Think of it this way: a vague request gets a vague response, but a detailed prompt with clear intent delivers exactly what you need.

Perplexity searches the live web in real time, so you always get up-to-date information with clear sources. The key to great results? Be specific. ​

Tips for searching

  • Start with a clear question. Instead of typing a single word like “inflation,” try “What is the current inflation rate in the US?”
  • Add context. The more background you give, the better. For example: “I’m a first-time homebuyer in the US — what federal programs are available to help me?”
  • Use keywords that matter. Include key terms that help focus the response. For example: “best noise-cancelling headphones under $200 for commuting.”
  • Say how you want the answer. You can ask for a summary, a table, bullet points, or a step-by-step guide — just include that in your prompt.

Examples

  • ✅ “Compare the pros and cons of hybrid vs. fully electric cars for city driving.”
  • ✅ “What are the latest CDC guidelines on vitamin D supplementation for adults?”
  • ❌ “Cars” (too vague — Perplexity won’t know what you’re looking for) ​

Deep Research

Need more than a quick answer? Research mode digs deeper — it searches multiple sources, reads through documents, and pieces together a comprehensive analysis for you. Think of it as having a research assistant that works in minutes, not days. ​

Tips for deep research prompts
  • Be clear about what you want to learn. A focused question leads to a focused report. For example: “What are the key trends in remote work adoption across the United States since 2020?”
  • Ask follow-up questions. Research mode works great in conversation. Start broad, then drill down: “Now focus on the impact on employee productivity.”
  • Upload your own files. You can attach PDFs, documents, or data and ask Perplexity to analyse them alongside web sources. For example: “Based on the attached financial report and latest market data, what’s the outlook for this company?”
  • Combine sources. Try prompts like: “Cross-reference my uploaded lecture notes with the latest studies on reinforcement learning and summarise the key takeaways.”
Examples
  • ✅ “Research the environmental impact of fast fashion and suggest sustainable alternatives, including cost comparisons.”
  • ✅ “Summarise the main arguments for and against a four-day work week, with supporting data.”
  • ❌ “Tell me about fashion” (too broad — narrow it down to get useful results) ​

Creating images

Perplexity can generate custom images right inside your conversation. Just describe what you’d like to see, and an image will be created for you automatically. ​

Tips for image prompts
  • Be as specific as possible. Describe the subject, style, colours, mood, and setting. The more detail, the better the result.
  • Mention a style if you have one in mind. You can ask for watercolour, photorealistic, minimalist, cartoon, vintage — whatever fits your vision.
  • Include context about how you’ll use it. For example: “Create a professional-looking banner image for a tech blog about AI.”
  • Iterate and refine. If the first result isn’t quite right, tweak your prompt and try again. Small changes can make a big difference.
Examples
  • ✅ “Generate a watercolour illustration of a cosy coffee shop on a rainy New York street, warm lighting, autumn colours.”
  • ✅ “Create a clean, minimalist logo concept for a sustainable clothing brand called ‘Evergreen.’”
  • ❌ “Make me a picture” (too vague — describe what you actually want to see)

Creating videos

You can generate short videos directly from a prompt. This is great for social media content, product demos, or creative projects. You can even upload a photo to use as the starting frame. ​

Tips for video prompts
  • Describe the scene and action. Think of it like writing a mini script — what’s happening, where, and what’s the mood?
  • Keep it focused. Generated videos are short, so aim for one clear concept per video.
  • Attach a reference image. If you have a photo you’d like to animate or build from, upload it with your prompt.
  • Think about the end goal. If you’re creating an ad or social post, mention that — it helps set the right tone and style.
Examples
  • ✅ “Generate a short video of a steaming cup of coffee on a wooden table with soft morning light, slow zoom in.”
  • ✅ “I want to sell my vintage desk lamp (photo attached). Generate a stylish video ad I can post on social media.”
  • ❌ “Make a video” (needs more detail about what the video should show) ​

Creating documents, presentations, and spreadsheets

Perplexity can create complete files for you — documents, slide decks, spreadsheets, and even simple web apps. Everything is backed by research with built-in citations, so your work stays credible. ​

Tips for document creation prompts
  • State the file type you need. Be explicit: “Create a Word document,” “Build a slide deck,” or “Make a spreadsheet.”
  • Describe the structure and content. For example: “Create a 10-slide presentation on our Q4 marketing strategy, with sections on budget, channels, and KPIs.”
  • Refine with follow-ups. After the first draft, use follow-up prompts to edit, add, or restructure. For example: “Add a conclusion slide” or “Make the executive summary shorter.”
  • Export when ready. Download your files as DOCX, PDF, PPTX, or XLSX — whatever you need.
Examples
  • ✅ “Create a professional PDF report on the current state of renewable energy in the United States, with charts and citations.”
  • ✅ “Build a spreadsheet comparing the top 5 project management tools by price, features, and user ratings.”
  • ❌ “Write something for me” (be specific about what you need and in what format) ​

General prompting best practices

No matter what you’re asking Perplexity to do, these principles will help you get better results every time:

  • State your intent clearly. Start by saying exactly what you want: “Create an image,” “Generate a video,” “Search for,” “Build a presentation.” This tells Perplexity what action to take.
  • More information = better results. The more detail you provide, the better your output will be. Include context, specifics, preferences, and constraints. A rich, detailed prompt produces a rich, detailed response.
  • Be specific, not vague. The clearer your prompt, the better the output.
  • Give context. Who are you? What’s this for? A little background goes a long way.
  • One thing at a time. Don’t cram multiple unrelated requests into a single prompt.
  • Say what format you want. Table, bullet points, report, slide deck — just ask.
  • Iterate. Your first prompt doesn’t have to be perfect. Refine and follow up.
  • Use plain language. You don’t need to speak like a machine. Write the way you’d explain it to a colleague. ​

Not at all. Just type naturally, like you’re asking a knowledgeable friend. Plain language works best. ​

What if my first result isn’t great?

That’s totally normal. Try rephrasing your prompt with more detail, or ask a follow-up question to steer the response. ​

Can I combine features in one conversation?

Absolutely. You can search for information, then ask Perplexity to turn it into a document or presentation — all in the same session. ​

Usage depends on your subscription plan. Pro and Max subscribers get higher limits for search, image generation, video creation, and document building. ​

All features are available on web, iOS, and Android. Some advanced features require a Pro or Max subscription.

Unlike traditional search engines, which make you sift through a list of links, Perplexity delivers the insights you’re looking for in one place. While we aim for accuracy, we encourage you to double-check sources for added confidence.

What is the difference between a prompt and a search?

In Perplexity, a prompt is a specific question or detailed instruction you give to the AI to generate a targeted response. A search is more like a traditional keyword-based query you’d use in a search engine to find information.

Learn about the differences between prompts and searches.

Which one works best with Perplexity?

Perplexity handles both prompts and searches smoothly. You can use simple keywords or detailed, conversational questions—both approaches will provide accurate results.

Pro tip

  • Use a prompt when you want detailed, conversational answers or need specific information (“Explain quantum physics to a beginner”).
  • Use a search when looking for quick facts or broader info based on keywords.

What’s the difference between typing a question and uploading a file or image?

Perplexity offers flexible ways to get answers, whether by typing a question, uploading a document, or using an image. Here’s how each option works:

Simply type your question into Perplexity as you would in a chat. Our answer engine understands the context of your query and searches the internet to provide a detailed, tailored response. This approach is perfect for general information, quick answers, or ongoing sessions where Perplexity remembers previous questions for more helpful follow-up responses.

Uploading a file or image

For document-based insights, upload a file like a PDF or image by dragging and dropping it into the search bar. This is ideal for summarizing content, analyzing code, or translating text within the file.

Privacy protection

Your privacy is a priority. Perplexity doesn’t store files indefinitely—uploaded files are retained for 30 days (7 days for Enterprise Pro users), allowing for follow-ups if needed. After this period, files are automatically removed from our system, but follow-up responses will still have context from previous questions in the conversation.
